```
1	"""Per-cycle run-log artefact.
2	
3	Each ``drain-cycle`` invocation produces a single JSON file at
4	``~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`` capturing one
5	entry per attempted issue. The filename embeds the run-start timestamp
6	(UTC, ``%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ``) so re-running ``drain-cycle`` against the
7	same cycle writes a new file instead of clobbering the prior one.
8	Downstream consumers glob the directory and group by ``cycle_id``
9	(carried inside each file).
10	
11	* KR1 (completion %) is computed from ``entries[].final_linear_state``
12	  merged across every file sharing the same ``cycle_id``.
13	* Cycle-drain self-grading reads every file across cycles.
14	* The kill condition (KR1 < 50%) merges per-cycle then thresholds.
15	
16	Schema:
17	
18	::
19	
20	    {
21	      "cycle_id":               "<linear-cycle-uuid>",
22	      "cycle_duration_seconds": <float>,
23	      "entries":                [
24	        {
25	          "issue_identifier":   "ABA-NNN",
26	          "started_at":         "<iso-8601 UTC>",
27	          "finished_at":        "<iso-8601 UTC>",
28	          "exit_code":          <int>,
29	          "final_linear_state": "Done" | "Todo" | ...,
30	          "worktree_path":      "<absolute path>",
31	          "halt_reason":        null | "<orchestrator stderr halt line>",
32	        },
33	        ...
34	      ],
35	    }
36	
37	``halt_reason`` is ``null`` on every Done entry (the agent flipped state
38	as required). On the orchestrator's halt entry it is the exact string
39	also written to stderr — both surfaces are produced by the same
40	``_halt_message`` helper in ``orchestrator.py`` so the on-disk and
41	terminal values cannot drift. Non-last entries are
42	``null`` by construction: the orchestrator returns on first halt, so
43	anything before the halt is a Done entry.
44	
45	``cycle_duration_seconds`` is computed automatically on every persist as
46	``max(finished_at) - min(started_at)`` across ``entries`` (``0.0`` when
47	empty). This is the KR2 grading proxy — "how long was the agent doing
48	execution for me unattended" stands in for "operator hands-off time" —
49	and replaces the originally specified ``time_spent`` block, which
50	required manual self-report at cycle close and was never going to be
51	filled in.
52	
53	Persistence is incremental: ``append_entry`` re-serialises the whole dict
54	on every call, so a mid-run crash still leaves a well-formed file with
55	every issue attempted so far. Cycle scale (≤ 15 issues) makes the cost
56	irrelevant.
57	"""
58	from __future__ import annotations
59	
60	import json
61	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
62	from datetime import datetime, timezone
63	from pathlib import Path
64	from typing import Any
65	
66	
67	def runs_dir() -> Path:
68	    """Return the directory where per-cycle run logs are written.
69	
70	    Resolved on every call (not module-import time) so tests can redirect
71	    via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`` after the module is imported.
72	    """
73	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
74	
75	
76	@dataclass
77	class RunLog:
78	    """Per-cycle run-log file. Construct once at orchestrator start.
79	
80	    Writing the initial ``{cycle_id, cycle_duration_seconds: 0.0,
81	    entries: []}`` shell in ``__post_init__`` means that even a zero-issue
82	    cycle (or a crash before the first ``append_entry``) leaves the
83	    artefact on disk, which is what kill-condition tooling needs to
84	    distinguish "drained nothing" from "never ran".
85	
86	    The filename embeds a UTC run-start timestamp with microsecond
87	    resolution, so two ``RunLog`` instances on the same ``cycle_id``
88	    (re-running ``drain-cycle`` after a halt) write to two separate
89	    files instead of one clobbering the other.
90	    """
91	
92	    cycle_id: str
93	    path: Path = field(init=False)
94	    entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list, init=False)
95	
96	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
97	        directory = runs_dir()
98	        directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
99	        run_started_at = datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc)
100	        timestamp = run_started_at.strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ")
101	        self.path = directory / f"{self.cycle_id}-{timestamp}.json"
102	        self._persist()
103	
104	    def append_entry(
105	        self,
106	        *,
107	        issue_identifier: str,
108	        started_at: str,
109	        finished_at: str,
110	        exit_code: int,
111	        final_linear_state: str,
112	        worktree_path: str,
113	        halt_reason: str | None = None,
114	    ) -> None:
115	        self.entries.append(
116	            {
117	                "issue_identifier": issue_identifier,
118	                "started_at": started_at,
119	                "finished_at": finished_at,
120	                "exit_code": exit_code,
121	                "final_linear_state": final_linear_state,
122	                "worktree_path": worktree_path,
123	                "halt_reason": halt_reason,
124	            }
125	        )
126	        self._persist()
127	
128	    def _persist(self) -> None:
129	        payload = {
130	            "cycle_id": self.cycle_id,
131	            "cycle_duration_seconds": self._cycle_duration_seconds(),
132	            "entries": self.entries,
133	        }
134	        self.path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
135	
136	    def _cycle_duration_seconds(self) -> float:
137	        if not self.entries:
138	            return 0.0
139	        starts = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["started_at"]) for e in self.entries]
140	        finishes = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["finished_at"]) for e in self.entries]
141	        return (max(finishes) - min(starts)).total_seconds()
142	
```

```
1	"""``drain-cycle`` CLI entry point.
2	
3	Zero-arg invocation drains the current Linear cycle. Each issue's target
4	repo is resolved from a ``repo:<name>`` label against
5	``~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml``; the operator runs ``drain-cycle`` from
6	anywhere, not from inside a target repo. The ``grade`` subcommand reads
7	the run logs and prints a health read.
8	
9	Secrets load before any module reads ``os.environ``, first hit wins:
10	shell-exported vars → ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` → the drain-cycle repo
11	root ``.env`` (dev-checkout fallback, absent once installed as a uv
12	tool). ``load_dotenv`` defaults to ``override=False``, so an
13	already-set var always beats a later source and the shell always wins.
14	
15	``repos.yml`` is validated eagerly at startup so a broken config halts
16	exit 1 on stderr before any Linear traffic or run-log file is written
17	— there is no cycle yet to log against.
18	"""
19	from __future__ import annotations
20	
21	import sys
22	from pathlib import Path
23	
24	from dotenv import load_dotenv
25	
26	from . import grade, orchestrator, repos
27	
28	_REPO_ENV = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / ".env"
29	
30	
31	def _load_secrets() -> None:
32	    """Populate ``os.environ`` from the first ``.env`` that defines each key.
33	
34	    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` sits beside ``repos.yml`` and the run logs,
35	    so an installed tool finds its secret there. The repo-root ``.env``
36	    is a dev-checkout fallback only — once installed as a uv tool the
37	    package lives in an isolated env where that path has no ``.env``.
38	    ``$HOME`` is resolved per call so tests can redirect it.
39	    """
40	    load_dotenv(Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / ".env")
41	    load_dotenv(_REPO_ENV)
42	
43	
44	_USAGE = (
45	    "usage: drain-cycle              drain the current Linear cycle\n"
46	    "       drain-cycle grade        print health read from run logs\n"
47	    "       drain-cycle --help"
48	)
49	
50	
51	def main() -> None:
52	    _load_secrets()
53	    argv = sys.argv[1:]
54	    if not argv:
55	        try:
56	            loaded_repos = repos.load()
57	        except repos.RepoConfigError as exc:
58	            print(f"drain-cycle: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
59	            sys.exit(1)
60	        sys.exit(orchestrator.run(loaded_repos))
61	    if argv == ["grade"]:
62	        sys.exit(grade.run(grade.default_runs_dir()))
63	    if argv in (["-h"], ["--help"]):
64	        print(_USAGE)
65	        sys.exit(0)
66	    print(f"drain-cycle: unknown invocation: {' '.join(argv)}", file=sys.stderr)
67	    print(_USAGE, file=sys.stderr)
68	    sys.exit(2)
69	
```

```
1	"""Drain a cycle by iterating over its sorted Todo/Backlog issues.
2	
3	Halt-on-not-Done, the orchestrator-owned Todo→In-Progress transition, the
4	run-log artefact, and the inspectable-halt UX all live here. The halt-message
5	helper ``_halt_message`` is the single source of truth for the operator-facing
6	halt string — emitted both on stderr and into the run-log entry's
7	``halt_reason`` field.
8	"""
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import subprocess
12	import sys
13	from datetime import datetime, timezone
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	from . import linear, prompt, runlog, worktree
17	from .repos import RepoResolutionError, Repos
18	
19	_DONE_STATE_TYPE = "completed"
20	_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME = "In Progress"
21	_CLAUDE_CMD = ["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]
22	_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 3600.0
23	"""Outer cap on one spawned ``claude -p`` session. A session that exceeds
24	this converts to a halt — same exit-1 + run-log entry + Halt: stderr line
25	as any other halt — so a hung agent advances the cycle to operator
26	attention instead of stalling indefinitely. Sized for one long unattended
27	issue; raise locally if a single issue legitimately takes longer."""
28	_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY = "<unresolved>"
29	"""Worktree-path placeholder for the pre-spawn resolution-halt path.
30	No path has been chosen yet — the issue couldn't be mapped to a target
31	repo — so the run-log entry and stderr halt line carry this marker
32	rather than a misleading fake path."""
33	
34	
35	def _now_iso() -> str:
36	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
37	
38	
39	def _halt_message(identifier: str, state_name: str, worktree_path: Path) -> str:
40	    """Single source of truth for the halt UX.
41	
42	    The same string lands on stderr (the operator's grep anchor) and in
43	    the run-log entry's ``halt_reason`` field — so kill-condition tooling
44	    reads the same human-readable explanation the operator saw at halt
45	    time.
46	    """
47	    return f"Halt: {identifier} (final state: {state_name}) at {worktree_path}"
48	
49	
50	def _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
51	    issue_id: str, *, target_state_name: str, pre_revert_state_name: str
52	) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
53	    """Restore Linear state on halt; return ``(state_to_report, error_msg)``.
54	
55	    The orchestrator transitions issues Todo→In Progress before spawning the
56	    agent. When the run halts, that In-Progress flag leaves the issue outside
57	    ``_PENDING_STATE_TYPES`` so a re-run silently skips it. This helper
58	    reverses the transition and re-fetches to confirm.
59	
60	    On revert success, returns the refreshed state name and ``None``.
61	    On revert failure, returns ``pre_revert_state_name`` (the state the
62	    issue is actually still in, so the operator can find it) plus the
63	    exception message — non-fatal. A failed refresh after a
64	    successful revert falls back to ``target_state_name``, since we trust
65	    the mutation landed even if the read-back didn't.
66	    """
67	    try:
68	        linear.set_state(issue_id, target_state_name)
69	    except Exception as exc:
70	        return pre_revert_state_name, str(exc)
71	    try:
72	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue_id)
73	    except Exception:
74	        return target_state_name, None
75	    return refreshed["state"]["name"], None
76	
77	
78	def run(repos: Repos) -> int:
79	    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
80	    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
81	    issues = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)
82	    if not issues:
83	        print(f"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.")
84	        return 0
85	
86	    for issue in issues:
87	        identifier = issue["identifier"]
88	        print(f"drain-cycle: picked {identifier}: {issue['title']}", file=sys.stderr)
89	
90	        started_at = _now_iso()
91	        try:
92	            target_repo = repos.resolve(issue)
93	        except RepoResolutionError as exc:
94	            # Pre-spawn resolution halt: no Linear state was moved, so no
95	            # revert is attempted. The worktree path is the ``<unresolved>``
96	            # placeholder since no repo was chosen.
97	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
98	            halt_reason = (
99	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, Path(_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY))}"
100	                f" — {exc}"
101	            )
102	            log.append_entry(
103	                issue_identifier=identifier,
104	                started_at=started_at,
105	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
106	                exit_code=-1,
107	                final_linear_state=state_name,
108	                REDACTED,
109	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
110	            )
111	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
112	            return 1
113	
114	        try:
115	            worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
116	            # Orchestrator owns the Todo→In Progress half so the lifecycle
117	            # doesn't depend on the spawned agent's compliance. The agent
118	            # still owns the …→Done half via Linear MCP — see prompt.py tail.
119	            linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
120	        except Exception as exc:
121	            # Convert any pre-spawn failure into a recorded halt rather than
122	            # a traceback: write a run-log entry with the planned worktree
123	            # path, print the halt message, exit non-zero. Subsequent issues
124	            # are not attempted — same contract as a spawn-time halt.
125	            planned_path = target_repo / worktree.WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
126	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
127	            halt_reason = (
128	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, planned_path)}"
129	                f" — setup failed: {exc}"
130	            )
131	            log.append_entry(
132	                issue_identifier=identifier,
133	                started_at=started_at,
134	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
135	                exit_code=-1,
136	                final_linear_state=state_name,
137	                worktree_path=str(planned_path),
138	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
139	            )
140	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
141	            return 1
142	
143	        agent_prompt = prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)
144	
145	        try:
146	            result = subprocess.run(
147	                [*_CLAUDE_CMD, agent_prompt],
148	                cwd=worktree_path,
149	                check=False,
150	                timeout=_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
151	            )
152	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
153	            original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
154	            effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
155	                issue["id"],
156	                target_state_name=original_state_name,
157	                pre_revert_state_name=_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME,
158	            )
159	            halt_reason = (
160	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}"
161	                f" — claude -p exceeded {_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS:.0f}s timeout"
162	            )
163	            if revert_error is not None:
164	                halt_reason += (
165	                    f"; revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
166	                )
167	            log.append_entry(
168	                issue_identifier=identifier,
169	                started_at=started_at,
170	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
171	                exit_code=-1,
172	                final_linear_state=effective_state,
173	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
174	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
175	            )
176	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
177	            return 1
178	        finished_at = _now_iso()
179	
180	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue["id"])
181	        post_spawn_state = refreshed["state"]["name"]
182	        is_done = refreshed["state"]["type"] == _DONE_STATE_TYPE
183	
184	        if is_done:
185	            # Append unconditionally for every attempted issue.
186	            log.append_entry(
187	                issue_identifier=identifier,
188	                started_at=started_at,
189	                finished_at=finished_at,
190	                exit_code=result.returncode,
191	                final_linear_state=post_spawn_state,
192	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
193	                halt_reason=None,
194	            )
195	            worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)
196	            print(f"drain-cycle: {identifier} done; worktree removed.", file=sys.stderr)
197	            continue
198	
199	        # Not-Done halt: revert to the pre-halt state so a re-run picks
200	        # this issue back up instead of silently skipping it.
201	        original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
202	        effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
203	            issue["id"],
204	            target_state_name=original_state_name,
205	            pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,
206	        )
207	        halt_reason = _halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)
208	        if revert_error is not None:
209	            halt_reason += (
210	                f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
211	            )
212	        # halt_reason carries the same string also printed to stderr below
213	        # so the on-disk and terminal surfaces cannot drift.
214	        log.append_entry(
215	            issue_identifier=identifier,
216	            started_at=started_at,
217	            finished_at=finished_at,
218	            exit_code=result.returncode,
219	            final_linear_state=effective_state,
220	            worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
221	            halt_reason=halt_reason,
222	        )
223	        print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
224	        return 1
225	
226	    return 0
227	
```

Note the earlier `...073511Z` run halted on ABA-204 with *"no repo: label"* at 07:35; the label got added and the 07:38 re-run is now executing it. So it's on its first issue of this run, mid-flight.

## How to inspect a running drain-cycle

Live observability is thin by design — the run log only gets an entry when an issue **finishes**, so mid-issue `entries: []` is normal, not a failure. Use the process tree and the worktree instead:

```bash
# 1. Is it alive, and what issue is it on? (the prompt names the issue)
pgrep -fl drain-cycle

# 2. Is the worker doing work or wedged? (CPU time should climb)
ps -o pid,etime,time,%cpu $(pgrep -f 'claude -p')

# 3. What has the agent actually changed so far?
git -C /Users/anton/src/pde-skills/.worktrees/ABA-204 status -s
git -C /Users/anton/src/pde-skills/.worktrees/ABA-204 diff --stat
```

Plus **Linear** (the orchestrator flips the issue to *In Progress* before spawning, the agent flips it to *Done* on success) and the **run log** at `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle>-<ts>.json`, which fills in one entry per issue as each completes.

## One caveat on `drain-cycle grade`

`grade` reads only *finished* run logs, so it can't see the in-flight ABA-204. Right now it prints `completion: 0% / CONCERNING` — that's grading yesterday's two halts (ABA-225, ABA-227 left In Progress) plus this morning's missing-label halt, **not** the live run. Don't read that as the current run failing.

```
=== design-decisions.md (observability/logging mentions) ===
13:**Why the agent-self-update path.** The orchestrator can only observe *process exit*, not *task success*. A Claude session may exit 0 having done nothing useful, or exit non-zero having actually shipped — exit code is a poor proxy for "the issue is Done." Letting the agent assert Done in Linear forces it to make an explicit, observable claim about its own outcome, which is exactly the artefact we need to grade KR1 and trigger the kill condition. If this pattern proves unreliable, that's the initiative's kill condition firing — not a bug to paper over.
29:**Why worktree-per-issue.** Issues drift. An agent that misunderstands its task can leave the workspace in a broken state — half-applied edits, uncommitted files, branch in the wrong place — that contaminates every subsequent issue's starting point. The worktree gives each issue a clean, identical starting point regardless of what the previous one did, and preservation-on-halt (US-B) means inspectable debug state. The cost is filesystem space and a few seconds of branch setup per issue. Cheap.
31:## 4. Run-log is one file per invocation, not one file per cycle
33:Each `drain-cycle` invocation writes its own run-log file at `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`. The per-file schema is unchanged — `{cycle_id, cycle_duration_seconds, entries: [...]}` — and the `cycle_id` carried inside each file is how downstream readers group runs of the same cycle together.
51:**Missing-repo halt behaviour.** Same machinery as every other pre-spawn halt: write a run-log entry, print the `Halt:` line to stderr, exit 1, leave subsequent issues untouched. The new wrinkle is that resolution failures happen before any Linear state is moved, so they skip the post-spawn revert path (`_revert_to_pre_halt_state`). This is the difference from the existing setup-failure halt: that one happens after `worktree.add` or the initial `linear.set_state` attempt; the resolution halt happens before either. Either way, no revert is needed because no state was moved.
53:**`repos.yml` config errors are even earlier.** A missing or malformed `repos.yml` halts the CLI at startup, before any Linear traffic and before the run-log file is created. There is no cycle yet to log against, so the failure surfaces only on stderr. This is enforced eagerly in `cli.main` so the orchestrator never sees a broken config.
61:`drain-cycle` is installed via `uv tool install`, which puts the executable on `$PATH` in an isolated environment. The Linear API key is read from `~/.drain-cycle/.env` (shell-exported vars still win), beside the `repos.yml` config and the `runs/` logs the tool already kept there.
66:- *Publish to PyPI*. Lets anyone `uv tool install drain-cycle` by name, but buys a release-and-versioning burden — tagging, changelogs, a name on the index — that a single-operator tool doesn't earn. `uv tool install git+https://…` already covers install-from-anywhere with no release step.
75:- **Retry.** A halted issue is not retried automatically. The operator inspects the worktree and decides — fix, redo manually, or descope.

=== headings in design-decisions.md ===
1:# Design decisions
7:## 1. The spawned agent updates Linear itself
15:## 2. `--dangerously-skip-permissions` is accepted
23:## 3. Fresh worktree per issue, not a shared workspace
31:## 4. Run-log is one file per invocation, not one file per cycle
41:## 5. Each issue declares its target repo via a `repo:<name>` Linear label
59:## 6. Installed as a `uv tool`, with the secret read from `~/.drain-cycle/.env`
72:## Out of scope (deliberate)
```

```
1	"""Prompt builder for spawned ``claude -p`` sessions.
2	
3	The prompt is the entire contract between the orchestrator and the spawned
4	agent — there's no system prompt, no multi-turn loop. The four-segment
5	ordering below is load-bearing: the agent reads top-down, so context
6	(title + body) comes before instructions (preamble + tail), and the tail
7	line is last so it stays in the trailing-tokens window the model attends
8	to most strongly.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	
16	_TAIL = (
17	    "before marking Done: run /code-review-and-quality on the working-tree "
18	    "changes, fix Critical/Required findings, commit + push, then post a "
19	    "review-summary comment on the issue and transition to Done."
20	)
21	
22	
23	def build(issue: dict[str, Any], worktree: Path) -> str:
24	    title = issue.get("title", "")
25	    description = issue.get("description") or ""
26	    identifier = issue.get("identifier", "")
27	
28	    preamble = (
29	        "---\n\n"
30	        "Execution instructions:\n"
31	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
32	        "- Base branch: main\n"
33	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
34	        "before marking Done):\n"
35	        "  1. Run `/code-review-and-quality` against the working-tree changes.\n"
36	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
37	        "are at your discretion.\n"
38	        "  3. Commit and push to main.\n"
39	        "  4. Post a short review-summary comment on the Linear issue via "
40	        "`mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` (count of findings by severity, "
41	        "fixed vs deferred).\n"
42	        "  5. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
43	        '(state: "Done").\n'
44	    )
45	
46	    return (
47	        f"# {title}\n\n"
48	        f"{description}\n\n"
49	        f"{preamble}\n"
50	        f"{_TAIL}\n"
51	    )
52	
```

> AGENT

No prior decision blocks this, and the gap is now precise. Here's *why* you can't see progress today:

1. **The worker is silent until it finishes.** The orchestrator spawns `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions` with no `--verbose`/`--output-format` (orchestrator.py:21,146). In plain `-p` mode Claude emits only its *final* message — so an attached terminal shows nothing for the whole issue, then a blob.
2. **The orchestrator prints only 3 sparse lines** to stderr: `picked X`, `X done`, `Halt:`.
3. **The run log writes only on issue *completion*** — so mid-issue it's `entries: []`, invisible (which is exactly what threw us last turn).
4. **No way to inspect from a second terminal** or when launched detached.

So there are really two different things you might want, and they need different fixes. One note first: drain-cycle is installed as a `uv tool`, so editing this repo won't touch the *currently running* ABA-204 process — changes only apply after `uv tool install` reinstalls, on the next run. Safe to build now.

Let me get the scope right before I build anything.

```
Claude Code - starts an interactive session by default, use -p/--print for
  --fallback-model <model>                          Enable automatic fallback to specified model when default model is overloaded (only works with --print)
  --include-hook-events                             Include all hook lifecycle events in the output stream (only works with --output-format=stream-json)
  --include-partial-messages                        Include partial message chunks as they arrive (only works with --print and --output-format=stream-json)
  --input-format <format>                           Input format (only works with --print): "text" (default), or "stream-json" (realtime streaming input) (choices: "text", "stream-json")
  --max-budget-usd <amount>                         Maximum dollar amount to spend on API calls (only works with --print)
  --no-session-persistence                          Disable session persistence - sessions will not be saved to disk and cannot be resumed (only works with --print)
  --output-format <format>                          Output format (only works with --print): "text" (default), "json" (single result), or "stream-json" (realtime streaming) (choices: "text", "json", "stream-json")
  -p, --print                                       Print response and exit (useful for pipes). Note: The workspace trust dialog is skipped when Claude is run in non-interactive mode (via -p, or when stdout is not a TTY, e.g. piped or redirected output). Only use this in directories you trust. Settings files that fail validation are silently ignored in this mode (no error dialog is shown).
  --replay-user-messages                            Re-emit user messages from stdin back on stdout for acknowledgment (only works with --input-format=stream-json and --output-format=stream-json)
  --verbose                                         Override verbose mode setting from config
```

```
1	"""Per-issue target-repo resolution.
2	
3	The orchestrator used to be single-repo by construction: every worktree
4	landed under ``Path.cwd()``. Cycles in this workspace span multiple repos
5	by design — ``linear-workflow.md`` makes "Affected repos" part of the
6	six initiative-readiness fields, and the Ops slot deliberately holds
7	cross-repo maintenance issues — so each issue now carries a
8	``repo:<name>`` label and ``~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`` maps the name to
9	an absolute path on disk.
10	
11	Two distinct error types so the two halt paths can branch cleanly:
12	
13	* ``RepoConfigError`` — startup-time problems (missing file, malformed
14	  YAML, empty, wrong top-level shape). The CLI catches it before any
15	  Linear traffic, prints to stderr, exits 1, and does **not** write a
16	  run-log entry — there is no cycle to log against yet.
17	* ``RepoResolutionError`` — per-issue problems (no ``repo:`` label,
18	  multiple ``repo:`` labels, name not in config, resolved path missing).
19	  The orchestrator catches it as a pre-spawn halt: write a run-log
20	  entry, print the matching ``Halt:`` line, exit 1. No revert is
21	  attempted because no Linear state was moved yet.
22	"""
23	from __future__ import annotations
24	
25	from dataclasses import dataclass
26	from pathlib import Path
27	from typing import Any
28	
29	import yaml
30	
31	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml"
32	_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX = "repo:"
33	
34	_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT = (
35	    "create it mapping each repo: label to an absolute path, e.g.:\n\n"
36	    "  repos:\n"
37	    "    drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle\n"
38	    "    pde-skills:  /Users/you/src/pde-skills\n\n"
39	    "see docs/repos.example.yml for a template."
40	)
41	
42	
43	class RepoConfigError(RuntimeError):
44	    """``repos.yml`` is missing or malformed."""
45	
46	
47	class RepoResolutionError(RuntimeError):
48	    """An issue cannot be mapped to a target repo."""
49	
50	
51	def default_config_path() -> Path:
52	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
53	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "repos.yml"
54	
55	
56	@dataclass(frozen=True)
57	class Repos:
58	    """Loaded ``repos.yml`` mapping (``name`` → absolute ``Path``)."""
59	
60	    mapping: dict[str, Path]
61	
62	    def resolve(self, issue: dict[str, Any]) -> Path:
63	        labels = [
64	            label
65	            for label in issue.get("labels", [])
66	            if label.startswith(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
67	        ]
68	        if not labels:
69	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"no {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} label on issue")
70	        if len(labels) > 1:
71	            names = ", ".join(sorted(label.removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX) for label in labels))
72	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"multiple {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} labels: {names}")
73	        name = labels[0].removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
74	        try:
75	            path = self.mapping[name]
76	        except KeyError:
77	            raise RepoResolutionError(
78	                f'repo "{name}" not in {_CONFIG_DISPLAY}'
79	            ) from None
80	        if not path.exists():
81	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"resolved path {path} does not exist")
82	        return path
83	
84	
85	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Repos:
86	    """Read ``repos.yml`` and return a validated ``Repos``.
87	
88	    Tilde-prefixed paths inside the file are expanded against ``$HOME``.
89	    Every failure mode raises ``RepoConfigError`` with a message that
90	    names the on-disk problem so the operator can fix it without
91	    re-reading source.
92	    """
93	    if path is None:
94	        path = default_config_path()
95	    if not path.exists():
96	        raise RepoConfigError(
97	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} not found at {path}\n{_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT}"
98	        )
99	    try:
100	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
101	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
102	        raise RepoConfigError(
103	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}"
104	        ) from None
105	    if data is None:
106	        raise RepoConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is empty")
107	    if not isinstance(data, dict) or "repos" not in data:
108	        raise RepoConfigError(
109	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must have a top-level 'repos:' key"
110	        )
111	    block = data["repos"]
112	    if not isinstance(block, dict) or not block:
113	        raise RepoConfigError(
114	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'repos:' must be a non-empty mapping"
115	        )
116	    mapping: dict[str, Path] = {}
117	    for name, value in block.items():
118	        if not isinstance(value, str):
119	            raise RepoConfigError(
120	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: path must be a string"
121	            )
122	        mapping[str(name)] = Path(value).expanduser()
123	    return Repos(mapping=mapping)
124	
```

```
1	"""Thin wrapper around ``git worktree``.
2	
3	Each issue gets ``.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/`` branched off ``main`` per
4	README §3, used once, then removed on Done.
5	
6	``git worktree`` stderr is captured and surfaced in the raised
7	``RuntimeError`` on failure. The orchestrator's pre-spawn try/except
8	threads the message into the runlog's ``halt_reason`` so the operator
9	sees git's actual diagnostic (dirty tree, branch already exists,
10	missing ``main``) rather than just a non-zero exit code.
11	"""
12	from __future__ import annotations
13	
14	import subprocess
15	from pathlib import Path
16	
17	BASE_BRANCH = "main"
18	WORKTREE_DIR = ".worktrees"
19	
20	
21	def add(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> Path:
22	    """Create a worktree branched off ``main`` for ``identifier``.
23	
24	    Returns the absolute path to the new worktree.
25	    """
26	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
27	    _run_git(
28	        ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), BASE_BRANCH],
29	        cwd=repo,
30	    )
31	    return worktree_path
32	
33	
34	def remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:
35	    """Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`."""
36	    _run_git(["worktree", "remove", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)
37	
38	
39	def _run_git(args: list[str], *, cwd: Path) -> None:
40	    result = subprocess.run(
41	        ["git", *args],
42	        cwd=cwd,
43	        check=False,
44	        capture_output=True,
45	        text=True,
46	    )
47	    if result.returncode != 0:
48	        raise RuntimeError(
49	            f"git {' '.join(args)} failed (exit {result.returncode}): "
50	            f"{result.stderr.strip() or result.stdout.strip() or '<no output>'}"
51	        )
52	
```

```
1	"""Self-grade a series of drain-cycle runs.
2	
3	Reads every ``~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json`` produced by the orchestrator
4	and emits a human-readable health read:
5	
6	1. Per-cycle section: cycle_id, attempted count, integer completion %,
7	   and halted entries as
8	   ``<identifier>: (<final_linear_state>, <exit_code>)``.
9	2. Across-cycles section: trend label over the last ``_TREND_WINDOW``
10	   cycles + recurrent ``(final_linear_state, exit_code)`` tuples
11	   appearing in ≥2 of those cycles.
12	3. Verdict section: one of ``HEALTHY`` / ``WATCH`` / ``CONCERNING``
13	   against the most-recent cycle's completion %. Labels are general
14	   health bands — the operator interprets them against whatever kill /
15	   continuation rule they hold for the work being drained. No
16	   project-specific reminders are baked into the tool.
17	
18	Run logs are grouped by ``cycle_id`` because one cycle can produce
19	multiple files when ``drain-cycle`` is re-run against the same cycle
20	after a halt. Chronological ordering uses the earliest filename per
21	cycle.
22	"""
23	from __future__ import annotations
24	
25	import json
26	import sys
27	from collections import Counter
28	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
29	from pathlib import Path
30	from typing import Any
31	
32	from . import runlog
33	
34	_TREND_WINDOW = 3
35	"""Number of most-recent cycles considered by the trend + recurrent-tuple
36	analysis. Pinned here; not configurable by the CLI."""
37	
38	_HEALTHY_THRESHOLD = 80
39	_WATCH_THRESHOLD = 50
40	"""Verdict bands. Boundaries are inclusive on the lower edge:
41	≥80 → HEALTHY, 50–79 → WATCH, <50 → CONCERNING."""
42	
43	
44	def default_runs_dir() -> Path:
45	    return runlog.runs_dir()
46	
47	
48	@dataclass
49	class _Cycle:
50	    cycle_id: str
51	    entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list)
52	    earliest_filename: str = ""
53	
54	    @property
55	    def attempted(self) -> int:
56	        return len(self.entries)
57	
58	    @property
59	    def completion_percent(self) -> int:
60	        if not self.entries:
61	            return 0
62	        done = sum(1 for e in self.entries if e["final_linear_state"] == "Done")
63	        return round(done * 100 / self.attempted)
64	
65	    @property
66	    def halted(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
67	        return [e for e in self.entries if e["final_linear_state"] != "Done"]
68	
69	
70	def _collect_cycles(runs_dir: Path) -> list[_Cycle]:
71	    """Group run-log files by cycle_id, chronological by earliest filename.
72	
73	    A cycle can span multiple files (re-running drain-cycle on the same
74	    cycle writes a new file). Ties are broken by filename; in practice
75	    cycles never tie because each cycle has its own UUID, but the rule is
76	    held for determinism if two cycles ever did share a min-filename
77	    timestamp.
78	
79	    Malformed files — invalid JSON, missing ``cycle_id`` — are skipped
80	    with a stderr warning. The grade tool is the kill-condition gauge;
81	    one bad file should never block reading the rest.
82	    """
83	    files = sorted(runs_dir.glob("*.json"))
84	    cycles: dict[str, _Cycle] = {}
85	    for path in files:
86	        try:
87	            payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
88	            cycle_id = payload["cycle_id"]
89	        except (json.JSONDecodeError, KeyError, OSError) as exc:
90	            print(
91	                f"drain-cycle grade: skipping malformed run-log {path}: {exc}",
92	                file=sys.stderr,
93	            )
94	            continue
95	        cycle = cycles.setdefault(cycle_id, _Cycle(cycle_id=cycle_id))
96	        cycle.entries.extend(payload.get("entries", []))
97	        if not cycle.earliest_filename or path.name < cycle.earliest_filename:
98	            cycle.earliest_filename = path.name
99	    return sorted(cycles.values(), key=lambda c: (c.earliest_filename, c.cycle_id))
100	
101	
102	def _trend_label(percents: list[int]) -> str:
103	    """Strict-monotonic over the window → improving/regressing; else flat.
104	
105	    Single-cycle or empty windows are "flat" by definition (no direction
106	    can be inferred from one or zero points). Two-cycle windows still
107	    apply the strict-monotonic rule (50 → 60 is improving; 50 → 50 is
108	    flat because the inequality is strict).
109	    """
110	    if len(percents) < 2:
111	        return "flat"
112	    if all(a < b for a, b in zip(percents, percents[1:])):
113	        return "improving"
114	    if all(a > b for a, b in zip(percents, percents[1:])):
115	        return "regressing"
116	    return "flat"
117	
118	
119	def _recurrent_tuples(cycles: list[_Cycle]) -> list[tuple[tuple[str, int], int]]:
120	    """Tuples appearing in ≥2 of the supplied cycles, with cycle-count.
121	
122	    Counted per cycle, not per entry: if a tuple appears three times
123	    inside one cycle and once in another, it counts as 2 cycles (not 4).
124	    Sorted by descending count then by the tuple itself for determinism.
125	    """
126	    counter: Counter[tuple[str, int]] = Counter()
127	    for cycle in cycles:
128	        seen_in_cycle: set[tuple[str, int]] = set()
129	        for entry in cycle.entries:
130	            key = (entry["final_linear_state"], entry["exit_code"])
131	            seen_in_cycle.add(key)
132	        counter.update(seen_in_cycle)
133	    recurrent = [(tup, count) for tup, count in counter.items() if count >= 2]
134	    recurrent.sort(key=lambda item: (-item[1], item[0]))
135	    return recurrent
136	
137	
138	def _render_across_cycles(cycles: list[_Cycle]) -> str:
139	    window = cycles[-_TREND_WINDOW:]
140	    percents = [c.completion_percent for c in window]
141	    lines = [
142	        f"window: last {len(window)} of {len(cycles)} cycle(s)"
143	        f" (max {_TREND_WINDOW})",
144	        f"trend: {_trend_label(percents)}",
145	    ]
146	    recurrent = _recurrent_tuples(window)
147	    if not recurrent:
148	        lines.append("recurrent tuples: none")
149	    else:
150	        lines.append("recurrent tuples:")
151	        for (state, exit_code), count in recurrent:
152	            lines.append(f"  ({state}, {exit_code}) x {count}")
153	    return "\n".join(lines)
154	
155	
156	def _render_verdict(cycles: list[_Cycle]) -> str:
157	    most_recent = cycles[-1]
158	    pct = most_recent.completion_percent
159	    if pct >= _HEALTHY_THRESHOLD:
160	        label = "HEALTHY"
161	    elif pct >= _WATCH_THRESHOLD:
162	        label = "WATCH"
163	    else:
164	        label = "CONCERNING"
165	    return f"verdict: {label} (most-recent cycle completion: {pct}%)"
166	
167	
168	def _render_per_cycle(cycle: _Cycle) -> str:
169	    lines = [
170	        f"cycle_id: {cycle.cycle_id}",
171	        f"  attempted: {cycle.attempted}",
172	        f"  completion: {cycle.completion_percent}%",
173	    ]
174	    if cycle.halted:
175	        lines.append("  halted:")
176	        for entry in cycle.halted:
177	            lines.append(
178	                f"    {entry['issue_identifier']}: "
179	                f"({entry['final_linear_state']}, {entry['exit_code']})"
180	            )
181	    return "\n".join(lines)
182	
183	
184	def run(runs_dir: Path) -> int:
185	    cycles = _collect_cycles(runs_dir) if runs_dir.is_dir() else []
186	    if not cycles:
187	        print(
188	            f"drain-cycle grade: no run logs found at {runs_dir}",
189	            file=sys.stderr,
190	        )
191	        return 1
192	
193	    print("== Per-cycle ==")
194	    print()
195	    for cycle in cycles:
196	        print(_render_per_cycle(cycle))
197	        print()
198	
199	    print("== Across cycles ==")
200	    print()
201	    print(_render_across_cycles(cycles))
202	    print()
203	
204	    print("== Verdict ==")
205	    print()
206	    print(_render_verdict(cycles))
207	    print()
208	    return 0
209	
```

> AGENT

I have everything I need to design this cleanly. Key findings:

- **Streaming flag:** `claude --verbose` (the help confirms it: *"Override verbose mode setting"*) makes `-p` stream turn-by-turn activity as text. The orchestrator's `subprocess.run` already inherits stdout/stderr, so adding `--verbose` streams to the launching terminal with zero capture plumbing.
- **Repo name for the marker:** `target_repo.name` (e.g. `pde-skills`) — no signature change to `resolve`.
- **Critical constraint:** `grade.py` globs `runs/*.json` and parses each as a cycle. So the marker file must **not** live in `runs/` or it'll pollute grade output. It goes at `~/.drain-cycle/active.json`, one level up. Run-log schema stays untouched.

Let me look at the test conventions so my new tests match, then I'll set up the Linear issue and build.

```
1	"""Drain-the-cycle iteration test for the orchestrator.
2	
3	The walking skeleton covered one issue end-to-end. This test pins the
4	behaviour that *every* sorted Todo/Backlog issue is processed in order, each
5	in its own worktree, and that every worktree is removed when the spawned
6	session signals completion.
7	
8	What we substitute and why:
9	
10	* Linear: stubbed in-process. The orchestrator imports the linear module by
11	  attribute, so monkey-patching ``cycle_id`` / ``pending_issues`` / ``get_issue``
12	  on it is sufficient. We do **not** stub the GraphQL transport — the layer
13	  under test here is the orchestrator loop, not the wire format (the wire
14	  format is exercised separately).
15	* Spawned ``claude -p``: replaced with a real shell script via
16	  ``_CLAUDE_CMD``. The script writes the basename of its cwd (the issue
17	  identifier — that's how the orchestrator names worktrees) into a shared
18	  marker file. The stubbed ``get_issue`` reads that file to decide which
19	  issues are Done. This satisfies the spec's "no-op script that calls back
20	  into the stubbed Linear" — the callback path is the marker file rather
21	  than an in-process call, because the script runs in a separate process.
22	* Git: a real ``git init`` repo with one commit on ``main``. ``git worktree``
23	  is exercised for real; this is the cheapest way to be sure the orchestrator
24	  doesn't paper over a worktree problem.
25	"""
26	from __future__ import annotations
27	
28	import subprocess
29	from pathlib import Path
30	
31	import pytest
32	
33	from drain_cycle import linear, orchestrator, repos
34	
35	
36	def _issue(
37	    identifier: str,
38	    priority: int,
39	    sort_order: float,
40	    *,
41	    repo_name: str = "test-repo",
42	) -> dict:
43	    return {
44	        "id": f"id-{identifier}",
45	        "identifier": identifier,
46	        "title": f"Title for {identifier}",
47	        "description": f"Body for {identifier}",
48	        "priority": priority,
49	        "sortOrder": sort_order,
50	        "state": {"type": "unstarted", "name": "Todo"},
51	        "labels": [f"repo:{repo_name}"],
52	    }
53	
54	
55	def _init_repo(repo: Path) -> None:
56	    """Create a real git repo with one commit on ``main`` for worktree tests."""
57	    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-b", "main"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
58	    subprocess.run(
59	        ["git", "config", "user.email", "test@example.com"], cwd=repo, check=True
60	    )
61	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.name", "Test"], cwd=repo, check=True)
62	    (repo / "README.md").write_text("seed\n")
63	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", "."], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
64	    subprocess.run(
65	        ["git", "commit", "-m", "seed"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True
66	    )
67	
68	
69	def test_orchestrator_drains_every_issue_in_sorted_order(
70	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
71	) -> None:
72	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
73	    repo.mkdir()
74	    _init_repo(repo)
75	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
76	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
77	
78	    # Three issues — chosen so a "naïve" iteration order (input order) and
79	    # the priority sort disagree, proving the loop consumes the *sorted*
80	    # list rather than the unsorted one.
81	    raw_issues = [
82	        _issue("ABA-X", priority=3, sort_order=2.0),  # Medium
83	        _issue("ABA-Y", priority=1, sort_order=1.0),  # Urgent — runs first
84	        _issue("ABA-Z", priority=2, sort_order=3.0),  # High
85	    ]
86	    sorted_issues = linear._sort_pending_issues(raw_issues)
87	    expected_order = [i["identifier"] for i in sorted_issues]
88	    assert expected_order == ["ABA-Y", "ABA-Z", "ABA-X"]
89	
90	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
91	    done_marker = tmp_path / "done-identifiers.txt"
92	
93	    def fake_current_cycle_id() -> str:
94	        return "stub-cycle"
95	
96	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
97	        # Re-sort each call (mirrors the real client, which sorts the wire
98	        # response) and exclude anything the fake claude script has already
99	        # marked Done — so an accidental re-fetch can't re-feed the same
100	        # issue back into the loop.
101	        completed = _completed_identifiers(done_marker)
102	        return linear._sort_pending_issues(
103	            [i for i in raw_issues if i["identifier"] not in completed]
104	        )
105	
106	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
107	        issue = issues_by_id[issue_id]
108	        if issue["identifier"] in _completed_identifiers(done_marker):
109	            return {**issue, "state": {"type": "completed", "name": "Done"}}
110	        return issue
111	
112	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", fake_current_cycle_id)
113	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
114	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
115	    # set_state is exercised by tests/test_orchestrator_set_state.py; here it's
116	    # a no-op so this test stays focused on iteration order + worktree cleanup.
117	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
118	
119	    fake_claude = _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path, done_marker)
120	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
121	
122	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={"test-repo": repo}))
123	
124	    assert exit_code == 0
125	    # The script appends the worktree-basename (== issue identifier) on each
126	    # run, so the file contents are the exact processing order.
127	    assert done_marker.read_text().splitlines() == expected_order
128	    # Every worktree was removed on success.
129	    worktrees_dir = repo / ".worktrees"
130	    assert not worktrees_dir.exists() or not any(worktrees_dir.iterdir())
131	    # ``git worktree list`` should show only the main checkout.
132	    listed = subprocess.run(
133	        ["git", "worktree", "list", "--porcelain"],
134	        cwd=repo,
135	        check=True,
136	        capture_output=True,
137	        text=True,
138	    ).stdout
139	    assert listed.count("worktree ") == 1
140	
141	
142	def _completed_identifiers(marker: Path) -> set[str]:
143	    if not marker.exists():
144	        return set()
145	    return {line for line in marker.read_text().splitlines() if line}
146	
147	
148	def _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path: Path, done_marker: Path) -> Path:
149	    """A no-op stand-in for ``claude -p``.
150	
151	    Records the basename of its cwd (the issue identifier — orchestrator
152	    names worktrees ``.worktrees/<identifier>/``) into ``done_marker``.
153	    The stubbed ``get_issue`` reads this file to learn which issues the
154	    'agent' has completed.
155	    """
156	    script = tmp_path / "fake-claude.sh"
157	    script.write_text(
158	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
159	        f'printf "%s\\n" "$(basename "$PWD")" >> "{done_marker}"\n'
160	    )
161	    script.chmod(0o755)
162	    return script
163	
```

```
1	"""CLI dispatch tests for ``drain-cycle``.
2	
3	Pins the four dispatch paths in ``cli.main``: zero-arg → orchestrator,
4	``grade`` → grade.run, ``-h`` / ``--help`` → usage + exit 0, anything
5	else → usage on stderr + exit 2. The behaviour guarded against is the
6	old fall-through where a misspelled subcommand silently triggered a
7	real cycle drain.
8	
9	A fifth path: zero-arg must first eagerly load ``repos.yml`` and exit 1
10	(without invoking the orchestrator, without writing any run-log) when
11	the config is broken. That test sits below.
12	"""
13	from __future__ import annotations
14	
15	import os
16	from pathlib import Path
17	
18	import pytest
19	
20	from drain_cycle import cli, grade, orchestrator, repos
21	
22	_SECRET=[REDACTED]"
23	
24	
25	def _write_env(directory: Path, value: str) -> Path:
26	    directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
27	    path = directory / ".env"
28	    path.write_text(f"{_SECRET}={value}\n")
29	    return path
30	
31	
32	def test_load_secrets_reads_home_env(
33	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
34	) -> None:
35	    """An installed tool with no repo-root .env still finds the
36	    secret in ~/.drain-cycle/.env."""
37	    monkeypatch.setattr(os, "environ", dict(os.environ))
38	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
39	    monkeypatch.delenv(_SECRET, raising=False)
40	    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "_REPO_ENV", tmp_path / "nonexistent" / ".env")
41	    _write_env(tmp_path / ".drain-cycle", "from-home")
42	
43	    cli._load_secrets()
44	
45	    assert os.environ[_SECRET] == "from-home"
46	
47	
48	def test_load_secrets_shell_var_wins_over_home_env(
49	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
50	) -> None:
51	    """A shell export always beats the file (load_dotenv override=False)."""
52	    monkeypatch.setattr(os, "environ", dict(os.environ))
53	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
54	    monkeypatch.setenv(_SECRET, "from-shell")
55	    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "_REPO_ENV", tmp_path / "nonexistent" / ".env")
56	    _write_env(tmp_path / ".drain-cycle", "from-home")
57	
58	    cli._load_secrets()
59	
60	    assert os.environ[_SECRET] == "from-shell"
61	
62	
63	def test_load_secrets_home_env_wins_over_repo_env(
64	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
65	) -> None:
66	    """First hit wins: ~/.drain-cycle/.env is read before the repo-root
67	    fallback, so it takes precedence when both define the key."""
68	    monkeypatch.setattr(os, "environ", dict(os.environ))
69	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
70	    monkeypatch.delenv(_SECRET, raising=False)
71	    repo_env = _write_env(tmp_path / "repo", "from-repo")
72	    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "_REPO_ENV", repo_env)
73	    _write_env(tmp_path / ".drain-cycle", "from-home")
74	
75	    cli._load_secrets()
76	
77	    assert os.environ[_SECRET] == "from-home"
78	
79	
80	def _stub_no_op_orchestrator(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> list[bool]:
81	    called: list[bool] = []
82	
83	    def fake_run(loaded_repos) -> int:
84	        called.append(True)
85	        return 0
86	
87	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "run", fake_run)
88	    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "load_dotenv", lambda *_a, **_kw: False)
89	    monkeypatch.setattr(repos, "load", lambda *_a, **_kw: repos.Repos(mapping={}))
90	    return called
91	
92	
93	def _stub_no_op_grade(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> list[bool]:

96	    def fake_run(runs_dir) -> int:
97	        called.append(True)
98	        return 0
99	
100	    monkeypatch.setattr(grade, "run", fake_run)
101	    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "load_dotenv", lambda *_a, **_kw: False)
102	    return called
103	
104	
105	def test_no_args_dispatches_to_orchestrator(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
106	    called = _stub_no_op_orchestrator(monkeypatch)
107	    monkeypatch.setattr("sys.argv", ["drain-cycle"])
108	
109	    with pytest.raises(SystemExit) as exc:

129	@pytest.mark.parametrize("flag", ["-h", "--help"])
130	def test_help_flag_prints_usage_and_exits_zero(
131	    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
132	    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
133	    flag: str,
134	) -> None:
135	    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "load_dotenv", lambda *_a, **_kw: False)
136	    monkeypatch.setattr("sys.argv", ["drain-cycle", flag])
137	
138	    with pytest.raises(SystemExit) as exc:
139	        cli.main()
140	
141	    assert exc.value.code == 0
142	    captured = capsys.readouterr()
143	    assert "usage:" in captured.out
144	    assert "drain the current Linear cycle" in captured.out
145	
146	
147	@pytest.mark.parametrize(
148	    "argv_tail",
149	    [
150	        ["bogus"],
151	        ["grade", "extra"],
152	        ["grade", "--verbose"],
153	        ["--unknown"],
154	    ],
155	)
156	def test_unknown_invocation_prints_usage_to_stderr_and_exits_two(
157	    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
158	    capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str],
159	    argv_tail: list[str],
160	) -> None:
161	    """The old fall-through silently ran the orchestrator on `drain-cycle bogus`.
162	    That's a foot-gun — the orchestrator is destructive (creates worktrees,
163	    transitions Linear state). Misspellings must fail loudly."""
164	    forbid_orchestrator(monkeypatch)
165	    forbid_grade(monkeypatch)
166	    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "load_dotenv", lambda *_a, **_kw: False)
167	    monkeypatch.setattr("sys.argv", ["drain-cycle", *argv_tail])
168	
169	    with pytest.raises(SystemExit) as exc:
170	        cli.main()
171	
172	    assert exc.value.code == 2
173	    captured = capsys.readouterr()
174	    assert "unknown invocation" in captured.err
175	    assert "usage:" in captured.err
176	
177	
178	def forbid_orchestrator(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
179	    def boom(*_a, **_kw) -> int:
180	        raise AssertionError("orchestrator.run() must not be called on unknown args")
181	
182	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "run", boom)
183	
184	
185	def forbid_grade(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
186	    def boom(_runs_dir) -> int:
187	        raise AssertionError("grade.run() must not be called on unknown args")
188	
189	    monkeypatch.setattr(grade, "run", boom)
190	
191	
192	def test_zero_arg_invocation_eagerly_validates_repos_yml(
193	    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, capsys: pytest.CaptureFixture[str]
194	) -> None:
195	    """A broken/missing ``repos.yml`` halts the zero-arg
196	    invocation before any orchestrator call — no Linear traffic, no
197	    run-log file, exit 1 on stderr. Stubs orchestrator with a tripwire
198	    so a regression that defers validation until after the orchestrator
199	    starts (and consequently after Linear is hit) fails loudly here."""
200	    forbid_orchestrator(monkeypatch)
201	    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "load_dotenv", lambda *_a, **_kw: False)
202	
203	    def failing_load(*_a, **_kw) -> repos.Repos:
204	        raise repos.RepoConfigError("~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml not found at /nope")
205	
206	    monkeypatch.setattr(repos, "load", failing_load)
207	    monkeypatch.setattr("sys.argv", ["drain-cycle"])
208	
209	    with pytest.raises(SystemExit) as exc:
210	        cli.main()
211	
212	    assert exc.value.code == 1
213	    captured = capsys.readouterr()
214	    assert "repos.yml" in captured.err
215	    # Pure stderr surface — nothing on stdout for the operator to parse.
216	    assert captured.out == ""
```

82:### What a run looks like
84:```bash
87:# drain-cycle reads the current Linear cycle, fetches its Todo/Backlog issues,
88:# and works through them one at a time. Each issue's target repo is resolved
89:# from its `repo:<name>` label against ~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml.
93:# ABA-241 is labelled `repo:drain-cycle`, which `repos.yml` maps to
94:# ~/src/drain-cycle. The orchestrator creates a fresh git worktree under
95:# ~/src/drain-cycle/.worktrees/ABA-241/ branched off main, flips the Linear
96:# issue to In Progress, then spawns a `claude -p` session inside the worktree.
104:# PDE-12 is labelled `repo:pde-skills`, so this run lands in a worktree
105:# under ~/src/pde-skills/.worktrees/PDE-12/ — different repo, same machinery.
114:# The session exited but the Linear issue is still In Progress — meaning the
115:# agent didn't mark it Done. drain-cycle stops the run immediately so you can
116:# investigate. The worktree is left in place; the run log captures the same
117:# halt line you see on screen.

Mid-issue there is almost no signal:\n\n- The spawned `claude -p` worker runs without `--verbose`/`--output-format`, so in `-p` mode it emits only its *final* message — an attached terminal shows nothing for the whole issue, then a blob.\n- The orchestrator prints only three sparse stderr lines (`picked`, `done`, `Halt:`).\n- The run-log gains an entry only when an issue *finishes*, so mid-issue it's `entries: []` — invisible.\n- There is no way to inspect from a second terminal or when the run was launched detached.\n\nNet effect: the operator cannot tell a working run from a hung one without manually walking the process tree and the target-repo worktree.\n\n## Changes\n\n1. **Worker streaming** — add `--verbose` to the spawned `claude -p` command so its turn-by-turn activity streams to the launching terminal (the orchestrator's `subprocess.run` already inherits stdout/stderr, so no capture plumbing).\n2. **Active-issue marker** (`drain_cycle/progress.py`) — the orchestrator writes `~/.drain-cycle/active.json` just before each spawn (cycle id, run-log path, issue identifier/title/repo/worktree, started_at, index, total, orchestrator pid) and clears it as soon as the worker returns (try/finally, covers done/halt/timeout). Marker lives **above** `runs/` so `grade`'s `runs/*.json` glob never mistakes it for a run-log file.\n3. **`drain-cycle status`** (`drain_cycle/status.py`) — reads the marker from any terminal and reports the in-flight issue: which one, repo, elapsed, worktree file changes, and done/remaining this run. Detects a stale marker (orchestrator pid gone → crashed/killed) and a no-marker state (points at `grade`).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- `drain-cycle status` during a run shows the current issue, its repo, elapsed time, `git status --short` of the worktree, and done/remaining counts.\n- `drain-cycle status` with no run prints \"No active drain-cycle run\" and exits 0.\n- A stale marker (pid not alive) is reported as such, not as a live run.\n- The marker is written before the worker spawns and removed after it returns on every exit path (done / halt / timeout).\n- `grade` output is unaffected by the marker file (it does not live in `runs/`).\n- Run-log schema unchanged.\n- New tests for `progress`, `status`, the orchestrator marker lifecycle, and the `status` CLI dispatch; full suite green.\n\n## Files\n\n- `drain_cycle/progress.py` (new), `drain_cycle/status.py` (new)\n- `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` (marker write/clear + `--verbose` + index/total)\n- `drain_cycle/cli.py` (`status` dispatch + usage)\n- `tests/` (new tests), `README.md` (usage), `docs/design-decisions.md` (marker-location decision)",

```json
{
```

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"Active-issue marker for live progress inspection.\n\nThe orchestrator is blocked inside ``subprocess.run`` for the entire\n``claude -p`` window, and the run-log only gains an entry when an issue\n*finishes* — so mid-issue there is no on-disk signal of what is being\nworked. This module writes a single ``~/.drain-cycle/active.json`` marker\njust before each spawn and clears it as soon as the worker returns, so\n``drain-cycle status`` (run from any terminal) can report the in-flight\nissue, its worktree, and how long it has been running.\n\nThe marker lives beside ``repos.yml`` — one level *above* ``runs/`` — so\n``grade``'s ``runs/*.json`` glob never mistakes it for a run-log file.\n\nIt carries the orchestrator ``pid`` so a reader can distinguish a live run\nfrom a stale marker left behind by a crashed or killed orchestrator.\n\nSingle-marker by design: a single operator runs one cycle at a time, so a\nsecond concurrent run would overwrite the first's marker. That limitation\nis accepted rather than engineered around.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport os\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\n\ndef marker_path() -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Path to the active-issue marker.\n\n    Resolved on every call (not at import time) so tests can redirect via\n    ``monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)`` after the module is imported.\n    \"\"\"\n    return Path.home() / \".drain-cycle\" / \"active.json\"\n\n\ndef write_active(\n    *,\n    cycle_id: str,\n    run_log_path: str,\n    identifier: str,\n    title: str,\n    repo_name: str,\n    worktree_path: str,\n    started_at: str,\n    index: int,\n    total: int,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Record the issue about to be spawned as the active one.\n\n    ``index``/``total`` are 1-based position in the run's sorted issue\n    list, so a reader can show ``[2/7]`` and compute remaining work.\n    \"\"\"\n    path = marker_path()\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    payload = {\n        \"pid\": os.getpid(),\n        \"cycle_id\": cycle_id,\n        \"run_log_path\": run_log_path,\n        \"issue\": {\n            \"identifier\": identifier,\n            \"title\": title,\n            \"repo\": repo_name,\n            \"worktree_path\": worktree_path,\n        },\n        \"started_at\": started_at,\n        \"index\": index,\n        \"total\": total,\n    }\n    path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + \"\\n\")\n\n\ndef clear_active() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Remove the marker if present. Idempotent — safe to call when no\n    marker exists (e.g. double-clear across an except + finally).\"\"\"\n    marker_path().unlink(missing_ok=True)\n\n\ndef read_active() -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the marker dict, or ``None`` if absent or unreadable.\n\n    A malformed marker is treated as absent rather than raising: the\n    reader is an inspection tool, not part of the drain hot path, so it\n    degrades to \"no active run\" instead of crashing.\n    \"\"\"\n    path = marker_path()\n    if not path.exists():\n        return None\n    try:\n        return json.loads(path.read_text())\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError):\n        return None\n"
```

## Report: Token Consumption & Duration Analysis

Based on my complete read of `drain_cycle/prompt.py` and `drain_cycle/runlog.py`, here are the findings:

---

### A. PROMPT TEMPLATE AND EXPENSIVE OPERATIONS

**File:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/prompt.py`

**The complete prompt template** returned by `build(issue, worktree)` (lines 23–51):

```python
def build(issue: dict[str, Any], worktree: Path) -> str:
    title = issue.get("title", "")
    description = issue.get("description") or ""
    identifier = issue.get("identifier", "")

    preamble = (
        "---\n\n"
        "Execution instructions:\n"
        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
        "- Base branch: main\n"
        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
        "before marking Done):\n"
        "  1. Run `/code-review-and-quality` against the working-tree changes.\n"
        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
        "are at your discretion.\n"
        "  3. Commit and push to main.\n"
        "  4. Post a short review-summary comment on the Linear issue via "
        "`mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` (count of findings by severity, "
        "fixed vs deferred).\n"
        "  5. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
        '(state: "Done").\n'
    )

    return (
        f"# {title}\n\n"
        f"{description}\n\n"
        f"{preamble}\n"
        f"{_TAIL}\n"
    )
```

**The tail line** (lines 16–20):
```python
_TAIL = (
    "before marking Done: run /code-review-and-quality on the working-tree "
    "changes, fix Critical/Required findings, commit + push, then post a "
    "review-summary comment on the issue and transition to Done."
)
```

**Expensive operations per issue:**

1. **Lines 35 / preamble step 1 + tail line:** The worker is instructed to **run `/code-review-and-quality` skill** on working-tree changes. This is a multi-axis code review skill that:
   - Analyzes the full changeset
   - May require examining files, tests, and implementations
   - Uses an external agent (skill invocation = potential additional agent spawning or recursive Claude calls)
   - Runs **before** any commit, so every issue does this unconditionally

2. **Lines 36–37 / preamble step 2:** Fix Critical/Required findings — requires re-reading code, potentially re-implementing logic, and re-running `/code-review-and-quality` until passing.

3. **Lines 38–41 / preamble steps 3–5:** Four sequential operations all involving Linear MCP calls:
   - `git commit + push`
   - `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` — save review summary
   - `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` — transition to Done

**No loop/restart logic:** The prompt does not instruct the agent to retry or loop. It's a one-shot linear sequence. However, the `/code-review-and-quality` skill itself may involve multiple internal fix/re-check cycles, and if the agent doesn't complete all steps, the orchestrator will halt (not retry per issue).

---

### B. RUNLOG SCHEMA AND FIELDS

**File:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/runlog.py`

**RunLog dataclass** (lines 76–94):
```python
@dataclass
class RunLog:
    """Per-cycle run-log file. Construct once at orchestrator start."""

    cycle_id: str
    path: Path = field(init=False)
    entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list, init=False)
```

**Per-entry dict shape** (lines 104–126, `append_entry` method):
```python
def append_entry(
    self,
    *,
    issue_identifier: str,
    started_at: str,
    finished_at: str,
    exit_code: int,
    final_linear_state: str,
    worktree_path: str,
    halt_reason: str | None = None,
) -> None:
    self.entries.append(
        {
            "issue_identifier": issue_identifier,
            "started_at": started_at,
            "finished_at": finished_at,
            "exit_code": exit_code,
            "final_linear_state": final_linear_state,
            "worktree_path": worktree_path,
            "halt_reason": halt_reason,
        }
    )
```

**Per-entry required fields:**
- `issue_identifier` (str)
- `started_at` (ISO-8601 UTC string)
- `finished_at` (ISO-8601 UTC string)
- `exit_code` (int)
- `final_linear_state` (str, e.g. "Done", "Todo")
- `worktree_path` (str, absolute path)
- `halt_reason` (str | None; null on Done entries, populated on halt)

**Top-level JSON shape** (lines 129–134):
```python
payload = {
    "cycle_id": self.cycle_id,
    "cycle_duration_seconds": self._cycle_duration_seconds(),
    "entries": self.entries,
}
```

**Run-log directory** (lines 67–73):
```python
def runs_dir() -> Path:
    """Return the directory where per-cycle run logs are written."""
    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
```

Files are written to: `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`  
Timestamp format: `%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ` (UTC, microsecond resolution, line 100)

**Duration computation** (lines 136–141):
```python
def _cycle_duration_seconds(self) -> float:
    if not self.entries:
        return 0.0
    starts = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["started_at"]) for e in self.entries]
    finishes = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["finished_at"]) for e in self.entries]
    return (max(finishes) - min(starts)).total_seconds()
```

Duration is computed as `max(finished_at) - min(started_at)` across **all entries** — i.e., the wall-clock span from the first issue's start to the last issue's finish (the entire unattended session). Per-issue duration is not computed; individual durations must be derived from `finished_at - started_at` per entry.

**Token usage / cost tracking:** **NONE.** There is no field for tokens, cost, API usage, or any token-related metrics in the RunLog schema. The orchestrator and logging system are **completely opaque to token consumption.**

---

### C. REFERENCED MODULES

**Files that reference or use prompt/logging:**

1. **`/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`** (lines 1–227):
   - **Line 143:** `agent_prompt = prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)` — builds the prompt for each issue
   - **Lines 146–151:** Spawns `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions` with the prompt
   - **Line 21:** `_CLAUDE_CMD = ["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]`
   - **Line 22–27:** Single-issue timeout: `_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 3600.0` (1 hour hard cap per session)
   - **Lines 80, 102, 131, 167, 186, 214:** Calls to `log.append_entry(...)` to record outcomes
   - **Lines 154–176:** TimeoutExpired handling — halt after 3600s

2. **Linear module** (`/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/linear.py`):
   - Called by orchestrator before spawning (line 119: `linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)`)
   - Called after spawn (line 180–181: `refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue["id"])`)
   - The agent itself makes Linear MCP calls per the prompt, but those are **inside** the `claude -p` session, not orchestrator-visible

**Cost implications:**
- The only visibility into cost is the 3600-second timeout flag (line 22)
- No instrumentation of token usage in the orchestrator
- No per-issue token budget or logging
- `/code-review-and-quality` skill cost is entirely opaque — the orchestrator has no insight into whether it's a cheap lint check or a full multi-turn review agent

---

### DIAGNOSIS SUMMARY

The token quota exhaustion likely stems from:

1. **`/code-review-and-quality` is invoked per issue unconditionally** (preamble line 35, tail line 16–20). If this skill is a full-fledged multi-axis code review agent with its own sub-agents or extended reasoning, it multiplies token usage significantly.

2. **Fix cycles:** Step 2 of the preamble says "Fix any Critical or Required findings" — if the first review finds many issues, fixing + re-reviewing creates multiple passes through the skill, each expensive.

3. **No token budget or per-issue cap:** The orchestrator has no mechanism to track or limit token use per issue. The only hard cap is 3600 seconds (1 hour wall-clock time), which doesn't throttle token burn if the session is doing heavy compute.

4. **No logging of token/cost metrics:** The run-log records only wall-clock timing (`started_at`, `finished_at`). Without recording tokens consumed, exit token count, or cost per issue, there's no post-hoc observability into which issue(s) consumed the quota. The duration column (`finished_at - started_at`) is wall-clock time, not token time.

5. **Linear MCP calls inside spawned session:** The agent makes MCP calls to save comments and transition state (preamble steps 4–5). If the worker is running inside a `claude -p` session that itself is instrumented to log to an external service, those MCP calls might be re-invoking the Linear server in verbose/instrumented mode, multiplying network/parsing overhead.

## Precise Evidence-Based Map: Orchestrator Subprocess Supervision

### 1. **Exact Subprocess Invocation**


**Parameter breakdown:**
- **Command list:** `["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions", "<agent_prompt>"]`
  - `<agent_prompt>` is the full prompt string built by `prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)` (line 143)

- **`cwd`:** `worktree_path` (the git worktree for the issue)
- **`env=`:** **NOT passed.** The subprocess inherits the parent's entire environment (which includes `LINEAR_API_KEY` and any other secrets)
- **`stdout`/`stderr`/`stdin`:** **NOT captured.** All default to the parent's inherited file descriptors (typically terminal or parent process's pipes). The spawned `claude -p` writes directly to the orchestrator's stdout/stderr.
- **`check=False`:** Do NOT raise `CalledProcessError` on non-zero exit; the exit code is captured in `result.returncode` (line 190, 218)
- **`timeout=_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`:** 3600.0 seconds (1 hour) per issue

**Critical observation:** No token budget, turn limit, cost cap, or total wall-clock cap is present anywhere in the codebase. Search confirms (line 161 of this report): `_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` is the *only* timeout constant. There is **NO global budget** across the cycle.
