### 3. **Timeout Handling: Is the Child Actually Killed?**

**Location:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`, lines 152–177

**Exception handling:**
```python
except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
    original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
    effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
        issue["id"],
        target_state_name=original_state_name,
        pre_revert_state_name=_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME,
    )
    halt_reason = (
        f"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}"
        f" — claude -p exceeded {_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS:.0f}s timeout"
    )
    # ... log and return 1
```

**Process group behavior:**
- **`subprocess.run(timeout=...)` kills ONLY the direct child process** — NOT the process group.
- **No `start_new_session=True`** anywhere in the code (confirmed by grep on line 150).
- **No `os.killpg()`, `process.kill()`, or `Popen`** — only `subprocess.run()` (which is a wrapper around `Popen` that uses `communicate()` with a timeout).
- **Consequence:** Any grandchildren spawned by `claude -p` (e.g., MCP servers, sub-`claude` processes, background threads) are **NOT automatically killed** on timeout. They become **orphaned** and continue running after the parent times out.
  - The Python `subprocess` module does NOT kill the process group; it only sends `SIGTERM` (or `SIGKILL` on Windows) to the direct child via `Popen.terminate()` / `Popen.kill()` when `timeout` expires.
  - This is documented in the Python `subprocess` module: *"If the timeout expires, the child process will be killed and waited for. The TimeoutExpired exception will be re-raised after the child process has terminated."* — but this is the *direct child only*.

---

### 4. **Full Loop Structure: Iteration, Halts, Reversion, Continuation**

**Location:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`, lines 78–226

```python
def run(repos: Repos) -> int:
    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
    issues = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)  # Fetched once at start
    if not issues:
        print(f"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.")
        return 0

    for issue in issues:  # ← Single pass over the sorted list
        identifier = issue["identifier"]
        print(f"drain-cycle: picked {identifier}: {issue['title']}", file=sys.stderr)
        started_at = _now_iso()
```

**Halt paths (all return 1, halting the entire cycle):**

| Halt Type | Lines | Trigger | Revert? | Next Issue? |
|-----------|-------|---------|--------|------------|
| **Resolution-fail** | 92–112 | `repos.resolve(issue)` raises `RepoResolutionError` | No (state never moved) | **RETURN 1** — cycle halts |
| **Setup-fail** | 114–141 | `worktree.add()` or `linear.set_state()` raises exception | No (state moved but catch reverts pre-revert attempt) | **RETURN 1** — cycle halts |
| **Timeout-expire** | 152–177 | `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` after 3600s | **YES** (lines 154–158) to original state | **RETURN 1** — cycle halts |
| **Not-Done** | 199–224 | `refreshed["state"]["type"] != "completed"` post-spawn | **YES** (lines 202–206) to original state | **RETURN 1** — cycle halts |
| **Done** | 184–197 | `refreshed["state"]["type"] == "completed"` | N/A (worktree cleaned up) | **`continue`** to next issue |

**`_revert_to_pre_halt_state()` behavior (lines 50–75):**

```python
def _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
    issue_id: str, *, target_state_name: str, pre_revert_state_name: str
) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
    """Restore Linear state on halt; return ``(state_to_report, error_msg)``.
    
    On revert success, returns the refreshed state name and ``None``.
    On revert failure, returns ``pre_revert_state_name`` (the state the
    issue is actually still in) plus the exception message — non-fatal.
    """
    try:
        linear.set_state(issue_id, target_state_name)  # Revert to original
    except Exception as exc:
        return pre_revert_state_name, str(exc)
    try:
        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue_id)  # Confirm the revert
    except Exception:
        return target_state_name, None  # Trust the mutation
    return refreshed["state"]["name"], None
```

**Loop structure:**
- Issues are fetched **once at the start** (line 81): `issues = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)`
- The loop iterates in a **single pass** over this list.
- **On any halt, the orchestrator returns 1 immediately** — the loop does NOT advance to the next issue.
- **Only on Done does the loop continue** (line 197).
- **No retry logic** — a halt is final for that cycle run.

**Implication:** A cycle can theoretically run for `N * 3600` seconds (where N = number of pending issues), with no upper bound. If 15 issues each hit the timeout, the cycle runs for 15 hours unattended with no circuit breaker.

**Search result:** None.

**Evidence:**
- Lines 145–151: `subprocess.run()` is called **exactly once per issue** with no retry loop.
- Lines 152–177 (timeout handler) and lines 199–224 (not-Done handler) both **return 1** (halt), not retry.
- No `for attempt in range()` or similar retry mechanism anywhere.

**Conclusion:** Zero retries. A spawn failure (timeout, exit non-zero, exception) is a halt-and-return.

---

## Summary Table: Critical Runaway Risk Factors

| Factor | Status | Risk |
|--------|--------|------|
| Per-issue timeout | ✓ 3600s (1 hour) | Moderate (can be long) |
| Cycle-level timeout | ✗ None | **HIGH** |
| Token budget | ✗ None | **HIGH** |
| Turn limit | ✗ None | **HIGH** |
| Cost cap | ✗ None | **HIGH** |
| Process group kill on timeout | ✗ (direct child only) | **HIGH** (grandchildren orphaned) |
| Retry logic | ✗ None | Low (no infinite loops) |
| Env isolation | ✗ (inherited) | Moderate (secrets exposed) |
| Stdout/stderr capture | ✗ (inherited) | Low (visibility maintained) |

---

## Diagnosis: Where Runaway Tokens/Time Likely Occur

1. **Per-issue timeout is 1 hour with no cycle cap:** A single pathological `claude -p` session can run up to 3600 seconds (e.g., spinning on a retrieval loop, retrying on rate-limit, looping within its internal agent loop) before the orchestrator kills it.

2. **On timeout, grandchildren are orphaned:** If `claude -p` spawns MCP servers or sub-processes that are long-lived (e.g., `claude` recursive calls, Anthropic API polling loops, or persistent background tasks), they keep running *after* the timeout, continuing to consume tokens and CPU.

3. **No global budget:** The orchestrator has no circuit-breaker for total token spend, total API calls, or total wall-clock time across the cycle. A 15-issue cycle can theoretically run for 15 hours if each issue hits the timeout.

4. **Inherited environment + no token tracking:** The spawned `claude -p` has full access to `LINEAR_API_KEY` and any other secrets, and there is no instrumentation to track how many tokens it consumes before timing out.

## Investigation Report: Environment Variables and Configuration in Spawned Worker

Based on my exhaustive search of the `drain-cycle` repository, here are my findings regarding how the orchestrator spawns the worker and why plugins may not load:

---

### 1. **Subprocess Environment Variables — Full Parent Environment Inherited Unchanged**

**File:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`, lines 146–151

```python
result = subprocess.run(
    [*_CLAUDE_CMD, agent_prompt],
    cwd=worktree_path,
    check=False,
    timeout=_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
)
```

**Finding:** The `subprocess.run()` call does **NOT** pass an `env=` parameter. This means the child process **inherits the full parent environment unchanged**. Python's `subprocess.run()` defaults to inheriting the parent's `os.environ` when no `env` argument is provided.

No environment variables are stripped, modified, or explicitly set anywhere in the orchestrator or any calling module before this invocation.

---

### 2. **The `_CLAUDE_CMD` Constant — No Env-Modifying Flags**

**File:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`, line 21

```python
_CLAUDE_CMD = ["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]
```

**Finding:** The command is hardcoded to:
- `claude` — invokes the `claude` binary on PATH
- `-p` — prompt mode (single-turn, non-interactive)
- `--dangerously-skip-permissions` — skips permission checks

There are **no flags that disable plugins, MCP, settings, or configuration loading**. Notably, absent are:
- `--no-plugins`, `--no-mcp`, `--no-settings`, `--strict-mcp-config`
- `--settings` / `--mcp-config` (which would override config location)
- Any `--append-system-prompt` or system prompt overrides

---

### 3. **Working Directory — Inside a Git Worktree (Inherits Tracked `.claude/` Files)**

**File:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/worktree.py`, lines 21–31

```python
def add(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> Path:
    """Create a worktree branched off ``main`` for ``identifier``.

    Returns the absolute path to the new worktree.
    """
    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
    _run_git(
        ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), BASE_BRANCH],
        cwd=repo,
    )
    return worktree_path
```

Where `WORKTREE_DIR = ".worktrees"` (line 18).

**Finding:** Each issue runs in `.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/` — a `git worktree` created by `git worktree add -b <identifier> <path> main`. This worktree:
- **Is a sparse working tree sharing tracked files from the target repo**
- **WILL contain the target repo's `.claude/` directory** (tracked by git as part of the repo)
- **WILL contain the target repo's `.entire/` directory** if tracked

The orchestrator spawns the worker with `cwd=worktree_path`, so Claude Code will discover:
- `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` at the worktree root
- `.claude/agents/` if any exist
- `.entire/settings.json` and hooks if the entire.io integration is tracked

**For the drain-cycle repo specifically:** The worktree inherits these tracked files:
- `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/.claude/settings.json` — contains hooks for entire.io
- `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/.claude/settings.local.json` — contains MCP/Linear permissions
- `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/.entire/settings.json` — entire CLI settings

---

### 4. **Secrets and Environment Loading — Happens in Parent Only**

**File:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/cli.py`, lines 31–41

```python
def _load_secrets() -> None:
    """Populate ``os.environ`` from the first ``.env`` that defines each key.

    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` sits beside ``repos.yml`` and the run logs,
    so an installed tool finds its secret there. The repo-root ``.env``
    is a dev-checkout fallback only — once installed as a uv tool the
    package lives in an isolated env where that path has no ``.env``.
    ``$HOME`` is resolved per call so tests can redirect it.
    """
    load_dotenv(Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / ".env")
    load_dotenv(_REPO_ENV)
```

Called from `cli.main()` (line 52) before orchestrator invocation.

**Finding:**
- **Secrets are loaded into the parent process** (`drain-cycle` CLI) via `load_dotenv()` with `override=False` (defaults to this behavior)
- **Load order:** shell-exported → `~/.drain-cycle/.env` → repo-root `.env`, first hit wins
- **The child process inherits the parent's `os.environ`**, so `LINEAR_API_KEY` and any other shell-exported variables are passed through

No `.env` file is explicitly loaded or injected into the child's environment; it's all in the parent's inherited `os.environ`.

    key = os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_KEY")
    if not key:
        raise RuntimeError("LINEAR_API_KEY is not set")
```

**Finding:** The child process can access `LINEAR_API_KEY` via the inherited environment.

### 6. **Design Decisions — No Documented Env Handling or Plugin Behavior**

**File:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/design-decisions.md`

Searched the entire document for mentions of:
- Environment variables
- Plugin/MCP loading
- Settings discovery
- `--dangerously-skip-permissions` behavior

**Findings:**

**Decision 2** (lines 15–22) covers `--dangerously-skip-permissions` but focuses on blast radius, not how plugins are affected:

> Every spawned session runs with `--dangerously-skip-permissions`. The agent can run any tool on any path inside its worktree, including shell commands, file writes, and network calls, with no operator approval.
>
> **Why accepted.** The single-operator personal-product context: target repos are mine, the Linear workspace is mine, the machine is mine. The point of the tool is removing prompts; gating them defeats the purpose.

**Decision 6** (lines 59–71) covers `.env` handling but only for the parent process:

> Secrets load before any module reads `os.environ`, first hit wins: shell-exported vars → `~/.drain-cycle/.env` → the drain-cycle repo root `.env` (dev-checkout fallback).

No decision entry explicitly addresses:
- How the child process discovers `.claude/` config
- Whether plugins/MCP load in the spawned session
- Any known limitations of `--dangerously-skip-permissions` affecting plugin discovery

---

### 7. **Summary — Why Plugins May Not Load in the Spawned Worker**

**The Code Itself Is Not Responsible:**

- The orchestrator passes the **full parent environment** to the child
- The child runs in a **worktree that inherits tracked `.claude/` config**
- No environment variables are stripped, modified, or disabled

**Potential Root Causes (Speculation, Not in Code):**

1. **`claude -p` prompt-mode behavior:** The `-p` flag may have different plugin/config discovery or initialization logic than interactive mode
2. **`--dangerously-skip-permissions` interaction:** This flag may bypass hooks or MCP initialization (not documented in design decisions)
3. **Claude Code version/feature differences:** The child process may be running a different version or build of Claude Code
4. **PATH differences:** Plugins like `entire.io` may not be discoverable if the child's `$PATH` differs (though code doesn't modify PATH)
5. **stdin/stdout/stderr handling:** Prompt mode may not initialize interactive features that plugins depend on

**What the Code Shows:**

- No explicit env-stripping occurs
- No config-location overrides occur
- The full parent environment is inherited
- The `.claude/` directory is present in the worktree
- `--dangerously-skip-permissions` is the only special flag

---

| Subprocess invocation | `orchestrator.py` | 146–151 | `subprocess.run([*_CLAUDE_CMD, agent_prompt], cwd=worktree_path, ...)` |

| No env manipulation | `orchestrator.py` | 146–151 | **No `env=` parameter** → defaults to full parent inheritance |

---

**Conclusion:** The orchestrator code does not manipulate environment variables or disable plugins. The issue lies either in Claude Code's `-p` prompt-mode behavior, `--dangerously-skip-permissions` side effects, or runtime differences between the spawned and interactive sessions — none of which are visible in or documented by the drain-cycle repository code.

  --json-schema <schema>                            JSON Schema for structured output validation. Example: {"type":"object","properties":{"name":{"type":"string"}},"required":["name"]}
  --max-budget-usd <amount>                         Maximum dollar amount to spend on API calls (only works with --print)
  --mcp-config <configs...>                         Load MCP servers from JSON files or strings (space-separated)
  --mcp-debug                                       [DEPRECATED. Use --debug instead] Enable MCP debug mode (shows MCP server errors)
  --model <model>                                   Model for the current session. Provide an alias for the latest model (e.g. 'sonnet' or 'opus') or a model's full name (e.g. 'claude-sonnet-4-6').
  -n, --name <name>                                 Set a display name for this session (shown in the prompt box, /resume picker, and terminal title)
  --no-chrome                                       Disable Claude in Chrome integration
  --no-session-persistence                          Disable session persistence - sessions will not be saved to disk and cannot be resumed (only works with --print)
  --output-format <format>                          Output format (only works with --print): "text" (default), "json" (single result), or "stream-json" (realtime streaming) (choices: "text", "json", "stream-json")
  --permission-mode <mode>                          Permission mode to use for the session (choices: "acceptEdits", "auto", "bypassPermissions", "default", "dontAsk", "plan")
  --plugin-dir <path>                               Load a plugin from a directory or .zip for this session only (repeatable: --plugin-dir A --plugin-dir B.zip) (default: [])
  --plugin-url <url>                                Fetch a plugin .zip from a URL for this session only (repeatable: --plugin-url A --plugin-url B) (default: [])
  -p, --print                                       Print response and exit (useful for pipes). Note: The workspace trust dialog is skipped when Claude is run in non-interactive mode (via -p, or when stdout is not a TTY, e.g. piped or redirected output). Only use this in directories you trust. Settings files that fail validation are silently ignored in this mode (no error dialog is shown).
  --remote-control [name]                           Start an interactive session with Remote Control enabled (optionally named)
  --remote-control-session-name-prefix <prefix>     Prefix for auto-generated Remote Control session names (default: hostname)
  --replay-user-messages                            Re-emit user messages from stdin back on stdout for acknowledgment (only works with --input-format=stream-json and --output-format=stream-json)
  -r, --resume [value]                              Resume a conversation by session ID, or open interactive picker with optional search term
  --session-id <uuid>                               Use a specific session ID for the conversation (must be a valid UUID)
  --setting-sources <sources>                       Comma-separated list of setting sources to load (user, project, local).
  --settings <file-or-json>                         Path to a settings JSON file or a JSON string to load additional settings from
  --strict-mcp-config                               Only use MCP servers from --mcp-config, ignoring all other MCP configurations
  --system-prompt <prompt>                          System prompt to use for the session
  --tmux                                            Create a tmux session for the worktree (requires --worktree). Uses iTerm2 native panes when available; use --tmux=classic for traditional tmux.
  --tools <tools...>                                Specify the list of available tools from the built-in set. Use "" to disable all tools, "default" to use all tools, or specify tool names (e.g. "Bash,Edit,Read").
  --verbose                                         Override verbose mode setting from config
  -v, --version                                     Output the version number
  -w, --worktree [name]                             Create a new git worktree for this session (optionally specify a name)

> AGENT

The token burn is now quantified and damning. Across the 5 issues, all on **Opus** (`claude-opus-4-7`):

| Issue | Turns | Cache-read tokens | Output |
|---|---|---|---|
| ABA-204 | 113 | 16.5M | 270K |
| ABA-205 | 234 | **43.1M** | 273K |
| ABA-134 | 192 | 34.1M | 341K |
| ABA-188 | 49 | 2.7M | 36K |
| ABA-133 | 98 | 11.3M | 141K |
| **Total** | **686** | **~107.7M** | **~1.06M** |

**~108M tokens of cache-read on Opus in 77 minutes** — that's the quota. The driver is **turn count** (234 turns on one issue): every turn re-reads the full accumulated context, and each session reloads the entire global CLAUDE.md rule-import chain fresh. The `/code-review-and-quality` skill + fix loops inflate turns. And `entire` is a standalone binary not registered as a user hook/plugin — so headless `-p` never invoked its checkpointing.

Let me confirm the exact orchestrator/runlog structure before designing.

1	"""Drain a cycle by iterating over its sorted Todo/Backlog issues.
2	
3	Halt-on-not-Done, the orchestrator-owned Todo→In-Progress transition, the
4	run-log artefact, and the inspectable-halt UX all live here. The halt-message
5	helper ``_halt_message`` is the single source of truth for the operator-facing
6	halt string — emitted both on stderr and into the run-log entry's
7	``halt_reason`` field.
8	"""
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import subprocess
12	import sys
13	from datetime import datetime, timezone
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	from . import linear, prompt, runlog, worktree
17	from .repos import RepoResolutionError, Repos
18	
19	_DONE_STATE_TYPE = "completed"
20	_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME = "In Progress"
21	_CLAUDE_CMD = ["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]
22	_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 3600.0
23	"""Outer cap on one spawned ``claude -p`` session. A session that exceeds
24	this converts to a halt — same exit-1 + run-log entry + Halt: stderr line
25	as any other halt — so a hung agent advances the cycle to operator
26	attention instead of stalling indefinitely. Sized for one long unattended
27	issue; raise locally if a single issue legitimately takes longer."""
28	_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY = "<unresolved>"
29	"""Worktree-path placeholder for the pre-spawn resolution-halt path.
30	No path has been chosen yet — the issue couldn't be mapped to a target
31	repo — so the run-log entry and stderr halt line carry this marker
32	rather than a misleading fake path."""
33	
34	
35	def _now_iso() -> str:
36	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
37	
38	
39	def _halt_message(identifier: str, state_name: str, worktree_path: Path) -> str:
40	    """Single source of truth for the halt UX.
41	
42	    The same string lands on stderr (the operator's grep anchor) and in
43	    the run-log entry's ``halt_reason`` field — so kill-condition tooling
44	    reads the same human-readable explanation the operator saw at halt
45	    time.
46	    """
47	    return f"Halt: {identifier} (final state: {state_name}) at {worktree_path}"
48	
49	
50	def _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
51	    issue_id: str, *, target_state_name: str, pre_revert_state_name: str
52	) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
53	    """Restore Linear state on halt; return ``(state_to_report, error_msg)``.
54	
55	    The orchestrator transitions issues Todo→In Progress before spawning the
56	    agent. When the run halts, that In-Progress flag leaves the issue outside
57	    ``_PENDING_STATE_TYPES`` so a re-run silently skips it. This helper
58	    reverses the transition and re-fetches to confirm.
59	
60	    On revert success, returns the refreshed state name and ``None``.
61	    On revert failure, returns ``pre_revert_state_name`` (the state the
62	    issue is actually still in, so the operator can find it) plus the
63	    exception message — non-fatal. A failed refresh after a
64	    successful revert falls back to ``target_state_name``, since we trust
65	    the mutation landed even if the read-back didn't.
66	    """
67	    try:
68	        linear.set_state(issue_id, target_state_name)
69	    except Exception as exc:
70	        return pre_revert_state_name, str(exc)
71	    try:
72	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue_id)
73	    except Exception:
74	        return target_state_name, None
75	    return refreshed["state"]["name"], None
76	
77	
78	def run(repos: Repos) -> int:
79	    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
80	    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
81	    issues = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)
82	    if not issues:
83	        print(f"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.")
84	        return 0
85	
86	    for issue in issues:
87	        identifier = issue["identifier"]
88	        print(f"drain-cycle: picked {identifier}: {issue['title']}", file=sys.stderr)
89	
90	        started_at = _now_iso()
91	        try:
92	            target_repo = repos.resolve(issue)
93	        except RepoResolutionError as exc:
94	            # Pre-spawn resolution halt: no Linear state was moved, so no
95	            # revert is attempted. The worktree path is the ``<unresolved>``
96	            # placeholder since no repo was chosen.
97	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
98	            halt_reason = (
99	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, Path(_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY))}"
100	                f" — {exc}"
101	            )
102	            log.append_entry(
103	                issue_identifier=identifier,
104	                started_at=started_at,
105	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
106	                exit_code=-1,
107	                final_linear_state=state_name,
108	                REDACTED,
109	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
110	            )
111	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
112	            return 1
113	
114	        try:
115	            worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
116	            # Orchestrator owns the Todo→In Progress half so the lifecycle
117	            # doesn't depend on the spawned agent's compliance. The agent
118	            # still owns the …→Done half via Linear MCP — see prompt.py tail.
119	            linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
120	        except Exception as exc:
121	            # Convert any pre-spawn failure into a recorded halt rather than
122	            # a traceback: write a run-log entry with the planned worktree
123	            # path, print the halt message, exit non-zero. Subsequent issues
124	            # are not attempted — same contract as a spawn-time halt.
125	            planned_path = target_repo / worktree.WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
126	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
127	            halt_reason = (
128	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, planned_path)}"
129	                f" — setup failed: {exc}"
130	            )
131	            log.append_entry(
132	                issue_identifier=identifier,
133	                started_at=started_at,
134	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
135	                exit_code=-1,
136	                final_linear_state=state_name,
137	                worktree_path=str(planned_path),
138	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
139	            )
140	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
141	            return 1
142	
143	        agent_prompt = prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)
144	
145	        try:
146	            result = subprocess.run(
147	                [*_CLAUDE_CMD, agent_prompt],
148	                cwd=worktree_path,
149	                check=False,
150	                timeout=_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
151	            )
152	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
153	            original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
154	            effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
155	                issue["id"],
156	                target_state_name=original_state_name,
157	                pre_revert_state_name=_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME,
158	            )
159	            halt_reason = (
160	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}"
161	                f" — claude -p exceeded {_ISSUE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS:.0f}s timeout"
162	            )
163	            if revert_error is not None:
164	                halt_reason += (
165	                    f"; revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
166	                )
167	            log.append_entry(
168	                issue_identifier=identifier,
169	                started_at=started_at,
170	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
171	                exit_code=-1,
172	                final_linear_state=effective_state,
173	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
174	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
175	            )
176	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
177	            return 1
178	        finished_at = _now_iso()
179	
180	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue["id"])
181	        post_spawn_state = refreshed["state"]["name"]
182	        is_done = refreshed["state"]["type"] == _DONE_STATE_TYPE
183	
184	        if is_done:
185	            # Append unconditionally for every attempted issue.
186	            log.append_entry(
187	                issue_identifier=identifier,
188	                started_at=started_at,
189	                finished_at=finished_at,
190	                exit_code=result.returncode,
191	                final_linear_state=post_spawn_state,
192	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
193	                halt_reason=None,
194	            )
195	            worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)
196	            print(f"drain-cycle: {identifier} done; worktree removed.", file=sys.stderr)
197	            continue
198	
199	        # Not-Done halt: revert to the pre-halt state so a re-run picks
200	        # this issue back up instead of silently skipping it.
201	        original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
202	        effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
203	            issue["id"],
204	            target_state_name=original_state_name,
205	            pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,
206	        )
207	        halt_reason = _halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)
208	        if revert_error is not None:
209	            halt_reason += (
210	                f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
211	            )
212	        # halt_reason carries the same string also printed to stderr below
213	        # so the on-disk and terminal surfaces cannot drift.
214	        log.append_entry(
215	            issue_identifier=identifier,
216	            started_at=started_at,
217	            finished_at=finished_at,
218	            exit_code=result.returncode,
219	            final_linear_state=effective_state,
220	            worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
221	            halt_reason=halt_reason,
222	        )
223	        print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
224	        return 1
225	
226	    return 0
227	
```

```
1	"""Per-cycle run-log artefact.
2	
3	Each ``drain-cycle`` invocation produces a single JSON file at
4	``~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`` capturing one
5	entry per attempted issue. The filename embeds the run-start timestamp
6	(UTC, ``%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ``) so re-running ``drain-cycle`` against the
7	same cycle writes a new file instead of clobbering the prior one.
8	Downstream consumers glob the directory and group by ``cycle_id``
9	(carried inside each file).
10	
11	* KR1 (completion %) is computed from ``entries[].final_linear_state``
12	  merged across every file sharing the same ``cycle_id``.
13	* Cycle-drain self-grading reads every file across cycles.
14	* The kill condition (KR1 < 50%) merges per-cycle then thresholds.
15	
16	Schema:
17	
18	::
19	
20	    {
21	      "cycle_id":               "<linear-cycle-uuid>",
22	      "cycle_duration_seconds": <float>,
23	      "entries":                [
24	        {
25	          "issue_identifier":   "ABA-NNN",
26	          "started_at":         "<iso-8601 UTC>",
27	          "finished_at":        "<iso-8601 UTC>",
28	          "exit_code":          <int>,
29	          "final_linear_state": "Done" | "Todo" | ...,
30	          "worktree_path":      "<absolute path>",
31	          "halt_reason":        null | "<orchestrator stderr halt line>",
32	        },
33	        ...
34	      ],
35	    }
36	
37	``halt_reason`` is ``null`` on every Done entry (the agent flipped state
38	as required). On the orchestrator's halt entry it is the exact string
39	also written to stderr — both surfaces are produced by the same
40	``_halt_message`` helper in ``orchestrator.py`` so the on-disk and
41	terminal values cannot drift. Non-last entries are
42	``null`` by construction: the orchestrator returns on first halt, so
43	anything before the halt is a Done entry.
44	
45	``cycle_duration_seconds`` is computed automatically on every persist as
46	``max(finished_at) - min(started_at)`` across ``entries`` (``0.0`` when
47	empty). This is the KR2 grading proxy — "how long was the agent doing
48	execution for me unattended" stands in for "operator hands-off time" —
49	and replaces the originally specified ``time_spent`` block, which
50	required manual self-report at cycle close and was never going to be
51	filled in.
52	
53	Persistence is incremental: ``append_entry`` re-serialises the whole dict
54	on every call, so a mid-run crash still leaves a well-formed file with
55	every issue attempted so far. Cycle scale (≤ 15 issues) makes the cost
56	irrelevant.
57	"""
58	from __future__ import annotations
59	
60	import json
61	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
62	from datetime import datetime, timezone
63	from pathlib import Path
64	from typing import Any
65	
66	
67	def runs_dir() -> Path:
68	    """Return the directory where per-cycle run logs are written.
69	
70	    Resolved on every call (not module-import time) so tests can redirect
71	    via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`` after the module is imported.
72	    """
73	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
74	
75	
76	@dataclass
77	class RunLog:
78	    """Per-cycle run-log file. Construct once at orchestrator start.
79	
80	    Writing the initial ``{cycle_id, cycle_duration_seconds: 0.0,
81	    entries: []}`` shell in ``__post_init__`` means that even a zero-issue
82	    cycle (or a crash before the first ``append_entry``) leaves the
83	    artefact on disk, which is what kill-condition tooling needs to
84	    distinguish "drained nothing" from "never ran".
85	
86	    The filename embeds a UTC run-start timestamp with microsecond
87	    resolution, so two ``RunLog`` instances on the same ``cycle_id``
88	    (re-running ``drain-cycle`` after a halt) write to two separate
89	    files instead of one clobbering the other.
90	    """
91	
92	    cycle_id: str
93	    path: Path = field(init=False)
94	    entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list, init=False)
95	
96	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
97	        directory = runs_dir()
98	        directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
99	        run_started_at = datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc)
100	        timestamp = run_started_at.strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%S%fZ")
101	        self.path = directory / f"{self.cycle_id}-{timestamp}.json"
102	        self._persist()
103	
104	    def append_entry(
105	        self,
106	        *,
107	        issue_identifier: str,
108	        started_at: str,
109	        finished_at: str,
110	        exit_code: int,
111	        final_linear_state: str,
112	        worktree_path: str,
113	        halt_reason: str | None = None,
114	    ) -> None:
115	        self.entries.append(
116	            {
117	                "issue_identifier": issue_identifier,
118	                "started_at": started_at,
119	                "finished_at": finished_at,
120	                "exit_code": exit_code,
121	                "final_linear_state": final_linear_state,
122	                "worktree_path": worktree_path,
123	                "halt_reason": halt_reason,
124	            }
125	        )
126	        self._persist()
127	
128	    def _persist(self) -> None:
129	        payload = {
130	            "cycle_id": self.cycle_id,
131	            "cycle_duration_seconds": self._cycle_duration_seconds(),
132	            "entries": self.entries,
133	        }
134	        self.path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
135	
136	    def _cycle_duration_seconds(self) -> float:
137	        if not self.entries:
138	            return 0.0
139	        starts = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["started_at"]) for e in self.entries]
140	        finishes = [datetime.fromisoformat(e["finished_at"]) for e in self.entries]
141	        return (max(finishes) - min(starts)).total_seconds()
142	
```

> AGENT

Answers captured. The model answer adds a per-ticket override (Sonnet default, ticket can specify), and the guardrail answer reframes the whole thing: you're on a **subscription**, so tokens — not dollars — are your mental model, and there's a big metric gap I need to surface. Let me reconcile it before writing the plan.

Your manual sessions run ~66.8k tokens (entire.io). But my measurement of the drain showed ABA-205 at **43M cache-read tokens**. These aren't the same metric: entire's "66.8k/session" is **peak context occupancy** (how full the window got on its biggest turn), while the quota-burning number is **cumulative tokens summed across every turn**. A manual issue is one continuous session ≈ one context fill. A drain worker took **49–234 turns**, and every turn re-reads the whole context — so cumulative balloons to millions even though peak-context stays ~150-200k. That's the gap, and the fix is to show **both** (peak context + cumulative + cost + turns), with the kill threshold on cumulative tokens since that's what drains the subscription.

Let me write the plan.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/.claude/plans/reflective-waddling-journal.md",
  "content": "# drain-cycle: diagnose quota burn + add token/time guardrails & live observability\n\n## Context\n\nThe last unattended drain (`cycle 3fb7ba8a`, run `20260523T0738`) drained 5 issues in\n77 minutes and consumed the operator's entire token quota. Manual work on the same kind\nof issue costs ~10 min and a fraction of the tokens. The operator wants to (1) diagnose\nwhat went wrong, (2) be able to monitor a live run (elapsed, progress, tokens, cost),\n(3) have hard guardrails on time **and** tokens that kill the worker on breach, and\n(4) have all of it written into the run log. A secondary observation — some normal\nplugins (e.g. entire.io) didn't load in the headless workers — hints the spawn\nenvironment diverges from an interactive session in ways that weren't intended.\n\n## Diagnosis (evidence)\n\nToken usage reconstructed from the worker session transcripts\n(`~/.claude/projects/*worktrees*ABA-*/*.jsonl`), all on **`claude-opus-4-7`**:\n\n| Issue   | Turns | Cache-read | Cache-create | Output | Wall-clock | Outcome |\n|---------|------:|-----------:|-------------:|-------:|-----------:|---------|\n| ABA-204 |   113 |     16.5M  |       0.99M  |  270K  |     18m    | Done    |\n| ABA-205 |   234 |   **43.1M**|       1.23M  |  273K  |     23m    | Done    |\n| ABA-134 |   192 |     34.1M  |       1.33M  |  341K  |     21m    | Done    |\n| ABA-188 |    49 |      2.7M  |       0.50M  |   36K  |      3m    | Done    |\n| ABA-133 |    98 |     11.3M  |       0.58M  |  141K  |     11m    | halt    |\n| **Σ**   |  686  | **~108M**  |     ~4.6M    | ~1.06M |     77m    |         |\n\nRoot causes, in order of impact:\n\n1. **Cumulative tokens, driven by turn count.** Quota burn = tokens summed across *every*\n   turn. Each `claude -p` turn re-reads the whole accumulated context (cache-read), so an\n   issue taking 234 turns reads ~180-200k tokens × 234 ≈ 43M. The healthy cheap issue\n   (ABA-188) took 49 turns / 3.3M cumulative; the runaways took 100-234 turns.\n2. **Opus on every worker.** `claude-opus-4-7` is pinned in user config and inherited by\n   every spawn. Opus cache-read/output is ~5× Sonnet — the single biggest cost multiplier.\n3. **Heavy per-issue workload.** The prompt (`prompt.py`) tells every worker to run the\n   full `/code-review-and-quality` multi-agent skill + a fix loop + commit + push + Linear\n   calls — far more turns than a manual edit.\n4. **Zero observability.** The run log (`runlog.py`) records only timestamps and exit code\n   — no tokens, cost, turns, or model. The operator was flying blind (cannot tell a\n   working run from a runaway).\n5. **No token/cost/turn guardrail.** Only a 3600s/issue wall-clock timeout exists; nothing\n   caps tokens, cost, turns, or total cycle spend. `claude` *does* support\n   `--max-budget-usd` (v2.1.150) — unused.\n6. **Timeout kills only the direct child.** `subprocess.run(timeout=)` with no\n   `start_new_session` leaves grandchildren (MCP servers, sub-agents) orphaned and still\n   consuming on a timeout.\n\n**Metric reconciliation (important for threshold calibration).** entire.io reports the\noperator's manual sessions at ~66.8k tokens avg (~30k Sonnet, ~100k Opus). That figure is\n**peak context occupancy** (largest single turn), not cumulative. A manual issue ≈ one\ncontext fill; an autonomous worker = N turns × context. So the guardrail and display must\nshow **both**: peak-context (comparable to entire's number) *and* cumulative tokens (the\nquota-relevant number), alongside cost and turn count.\n\n## Decisions (from operator)\n\n- **Monitoring:** stream-json — parse `claude`'s event stream live; show progress and log\n  final usage.\n- **Worker model:** default `claude-sonnet-4-6`; a `model:` Linear label on the issue\n  overrides (mirrors the existing `repo:` label mechanism). Ticket wins when set.\n- **Guardrails:** kill on time **and** tokens; show cost + tokens together in context.\n  Subscription user → token count is primary, cost shown alongside.\n- **Plugin issue:** observability-first — capture what loads, add a reproduction step,\n  document the divergence; do **not** blind-fix the root cause this round.\n\n## Approach\n\n### 1. Verification spike (gate — do first)\nRun once, read-only, to ground the parser in real output (no assuming the schema):\n```\nclaude -p --verbose --output-format stream-json --max-budget-usd 0.50 \"say hi\"\n```\nCapture the exact event shapes: the `system`/`init` event, per-turn `assistant` events\n(`message.usage`: `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`,\n`cache_read_input_tokens`), and the terminal `result` event (`total_cost_usd`, `usage`,\n`num_turns`, `duration_ms`, `session_id`, `is_error`, `subtype`). Confirm `--verbose` is\nrequired with `stream-json` in `-p`. **Gate:** only build the parser against confirmed\nfields; flag any field that isn't present.\n\n### 2. Worker model resolution — `model:` label\nNew small resolver mirroring `repos.py`'s `repo:`-label extraction: read a `model:<alias>`\nlabel off `issue[\"labels\"]`, default `claude-sonnet-4-6`. Pass `--model <resolved>` into\nthe worker command. Unknown/missing label → default. Keep it a pure function for testing.\n\n### 3. Spawn refactor — `drain_cycle/worker.py` (new)\nExtract the spawn block (orchestrator.py:143-178) into a `worker.run_issue(...)` that owns\nthe streamed, guarded spawn and returns a result struct\n(`exit_code`, `usage`, `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `peak_context_tokens`, `session_id`,\n`is_error`, `kill_reason`). Implementation:\n- `subprocess.Popen([claude, -p, --verbose, --output-format, stream-json,\n  --model, <m>, --max-budget-usd, <cap>, --dangerously-skip-permissions, <prompt>],\n  cwd=worktree, stdout=PIPE, stderr=STDOUT, text=True, bufsize=1, start_new_session=True)`.\n  `start_new_session=True` makes the child a process-group leader so we can reap\n  grandchildren.\n- Read `p.stdout` line by line; parse each JSON event. On each event: accumulate cumulative\n  usage + cost + turns, track peak context, update the live marker (step 5), emit a compact\n  progress line to stderr, and check guardrails (step 4).\n- A `threading.Timer` (per-issue time cap) + cycle-deadline check kills the **process\n  group** (`os.killpg(os.getpgid(p.pid), SIGTERM)` then `SIGKILL`) so a quiet long turn\n  still trips the time guard. Cancel the timer on normal completion.\n- Capture the terminal `result` event for the run-log entry.\n- `--max-budget-usd` is the native belt; the orchestrator-side cumulative-token + time caps\n  are the suspenders.\n\n### 4. Guardrails — `drain_cycle/limits.py` (new)\nThresholds with baked-in defaults, overridable by an optional `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`\nand/or CLI flags. Defaults (evidence-grounded, tunable — first real run will calibrate):\n- Per-issue: **8M cumulative tokens** · **20 min** · **$15** (`--max-budget-usd`).\n- Cycle-wide: **30M cumulative tokens** · **90 min** · **$60**.\nOn breach: kill the worker's process group, record a halt entry whose `halt_reason` names\nthe breached limit and the value at kill time, and stop the cycle (same exit-1 + revert\ncontract as existing halts). Each guardrail is independently on/off-able.\n\n### 5. Live observability — `drain_cycle/progress.py` + `status.py` (new)\n- `progress.py`: write/read/clear `~/.drain-cycle/active.json` (one level **above**\n  `runs/` so `grade.py`'s `runs/*.json` glob never sees it). Marker carries:\n  `pid`, `cycle_id`, `run_log_path`, `issue{identifier,title,repo,worktree_path}`,\n  `model`, `started_at`, `index`, `total`, and a live `progress` block\n  (`turns`, `cumulative_tokens`, `peak_context_tokens`, `cost_usd`, `elapsed_seconds`,\n  `last_event_at`). Updated on every stream event; cleared in a `finally` covering all exit\n  paths. `pid` lets `status` detect a stale marker (dead process).\n- `status.py` (`drain-cycle status`): read the marker and print a human view —\n  which issue, model, `[index/total]`, elapsed, turns, **tokens (cumulative + peak) and\n  cost shown together**, against the configured caps (e.g. `12.4M / 8M ⚠`). Live marker →\n  live view; dead pid → stale view; no marker → point at `grade`/run logs.\n- Orchestrator emits a compact per-event stderr line for terminal-watching, e.g.\n  `ABA-205 · turn 42 · 8.1M tok (peak 180k) · $12.30 · 14m`.\n\n### 6. Logging — extend `runlog.py` (additive, grade-safe)\n`grade.py` reads only `cycle_id` + `entries[].final_linear_state`, so new keys are\nbackward-compatible. Add to each entry: `model`, `usage` (input/output/cache_create/\ncache_read/cumulative + peak_context), `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `session_id`, `is_error`,\nexplicit `duration_seconds`. Add top-level aggregates: `cycle_cost_usd`,\n`cycle_tokens_cumulative`. Halt entries from a guardrail breach carry the breach detail in\n`halt_reason`. Update the module docstring schema block.\n\n### 7. Plugin / entire.io investigation (observability-first)\n- The spawn inherits the full parent env and runs in a worktree of the *target* repo;\n  `entire` is a standalone binary (`~/.local/bin/entire`), not in user `enabledPlugins` and\n  not a user hook — so headless `-p` never triggered its checkpointing. Plugins enabled at\n  user level *should* load under `-p` (only `--bare` skips plugin sync), so the divergence\n  needs runtime confirmation, not a guess.\n- Add an opt-in `--debug-file <path>` capture on the worker spawn (written next to the run\n  log) so the next run records exactly which plugins/MCP/hooks initialised.\n- Add a documented one-shot reproduction step (run `claude -p` headless in a worktree, diff\n  loaded plugins vs interactive) to `docs/design-decisions.md`, and a decision entry naming\n  the headless-vs-interactive divergence as a known constraint with the open question. No\n  code fix to plugin loading this round.\n\n### 8. Docs\n- `README.md`: document `drain-cycle status`, the `model:` label, the guardrail limits +\n  `limits.yml`, and the new run-log fields.\n- `docs/design-decisions.md`: entries for stream-json+Popen+process-group kill, the\n  token/cost/time guardrails and defaults, default-Sonnet + `model:` override, the\n  active-marker location (above `runs/`), and the plugin-divergence open question.\n\n## Files\n\n- **New:** `drain_cycle/worker.py`, `drain_cycle/progress.py`, `drain_cycle/status.py`,\n  `drain_cycle/limits.py`, plus a `model:`-label resolver (small fn, in `repos.py` next to\n  the `repo:` logic or its own `model.py`).\n- **Edit:** `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` (call `worker.run_issue`, index/total enumerate,\n  marker write/clear, cycle-deadline + cycle-token aggregate, guardrail-breach halt path),\n  `drain_cycle/runlog.py` (extra fields + aggregates + docstring), `drain_cycle/cli.py`\n  (`status` dispatch + usage), `drain_cycle/prompt.py` (only if model/guardrail context\n  needs surfacing to the worker), `README.md`, `docs/design-decisions.md`.\n- **Tests:** `tests/test_worker.py` (stream parse, guardrail kill via fake claude script\n  emitting stream-json, process-group kill), `tests/test_progress.py`,\n  `tests/test_status.py`, `tests/test_limits.py`, `model:`-label resolution test, run-log\n  new-field test, orchestrator marker-lifecycle + guardrail-halt test, `status` CLI\n  dispatch test. Reuse existing test conventions (`_issue(...)` helper, fake-claude shell\n  script via `_CLAUDE_CMD` monkeypatch, `monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", tmp_path)`).\n\n## Verification\n\n1. **Spike gate:** event schema confirmed against real `claude -p --output-format\n   stream-json` output before the parser is written.\n2. **Unit suite green:** `uv run pytest` — including a fake-claude script that emits a\n   scripted stream-json sequence and one that overshoots a tiny token cap to prove the\n   process-group kill fires and records the right `halt_reason`.\n3. **End-to-end (one real issue):** point drain-cycle at a single throwaway issue with a\n   deliberately tiny per-issue token cap; confirm (a) `drain-cycle status` shows live\n   tokens+cost+turns+elapsed from another terminal, (b) the worker is killed on cap breach\n   and its whole process group dies (no orphaned `claude`/MCP procs — check `pgrep -fl\n   claude`), (c) the run-log entry carries model/usage/cost/turns/session_id and the breach\n   reason, (d) the `--debug-file` capture shows which plugins loaded headless.\n4. **Calibration:** with real numbers in hand, confirm the default caps catch a runaway\n   like ABA-205 while letting a healthy issue like ABA-188 finish; adjust `limits.yml`.\n\n## Linear\n\nTrack under a single issue in the Autonomous-cycle-drain project (broader than the existing\nABA-234 \"status command + streaming\", which this **absorbs** — fold its scope in and close\nor repurpose ABA-234). On completion follow the repo workflow: `/code-review-and-quality` →\nfix Critical/Required → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done.\n"
}

Each `claude -p` turn re-reads the whole accumulated context (cache-read), so an\n   issue taking 234 turns reads ~180-200k tokens × 234 ≈ 43M. The healthy cheap issue\n   (ABA-188) took 49 turns / 3.3M cumulative; the runaways took 100-234 turns.\n2. **Opus on every worker.** `claude-opus-4-7` is pinned in user config and inherited by\n   every spawn. Opus cache-read/output is ~5× Sonnet — the single biggest cost multiplier.\n3. **Heavy per-issue workload.** The prompt (`prompt.py`) tells every worker to run the\n   full `/code-review-and-quality` multi-agent skill + a fix loop + commit + push + Linear\n   calls — far more turns than a manual edit.\n4. **Zero observability.** The run log (`runlog.py`) records only timestamps and exit code\n   — no tokens, cost, turns, or model. The operator was flying blind (cannot tell a\n   working run from a runaway).\n5. **No token/cost/turn guardrail.** Only a 3600s/issue wall-clock timeout exists; nothing\n   caps tokens, cost, turns, or total cycle spend. `claude` *does* support\n   `--max-budget-usd` (v2.1.150) — unused.\n6. **Timeout kills only the direct child.** `subprocess.run(timeout=)` with no\n   `start_new_session` leaves grandchildren (MCP servers, sub-agents) orphaned and still\n   consuming on a timeout.\n\n**Metric reconciliation (important for threshold calibration).** entire.io reports the\noperator's manual sessions at ~66.8k tokens avg (~30k Sonnet, ~100k Opus). That figure is\n**peak context occupancy** (largest single turn), not cumulative. A manual issue ≈ one\ncontext fill; an autonomous worker = N turns × context. So the guardrail and display must\nshow **both**: peak-context (comparable to entire's number) *and* cumulative tokens (the\nquota-relevant number), alongside cost and turn count.\n\n## Decisions (from operator)\n\n- **Monitoring:** stream-json — parse `claude`'s event stream live; show progress and log\n  final usage.\n- **Worker model:** default `claude-sonnet-4-6`; a `model:` Linear label on the issue\n  overrides (mirrors the existing `repo:` label mechanism). Ticket wins when set.\n- **Guardrails:** kill on time **and** tokens; show cost + tokens together in context.\n  Subscription user → token count is primary, cost shown alongside.\n- **Plugin issue:** observability-first — capture what loads, add a reproduction step,\n  document the divergence; do **not** blind-fix the root cause this round.\n\n## Approach\n\n### 1. Verification spike (gate — do first)\nRun once, read-only, to ground the parser in real output (no assuming the schema):\n```\nclaude -p --verbose --output-format stream-json --max-budget-usd 0.50 \"say hi\"\n```\nCapture the exact event shapes: the `system`/`init` event, per-turn `assistant` events\n(`message.usage`: `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`,\n`cache_read_input_tokens`), and the terminal `result` event (`total_cost_usd`, `usage`,\n`num_turns`, `duration_ms`, `session_id`, `is_error`, `subtype`). Confirm `--verbose` is\nrequired with `stream-json` in `-p`. **Gate:** only build the parser against confirmed\nfields; flag any field that isn't present.\n\n### 2. Worker model resolution — `model:` label\nNew small resolver mirroring `repos.py`'s `repo:`-label extraction: read a `model:<alias>`\nlabel off `issue[\"labels\"]`, default `claude-sonnet-4-6`. Pass `--model <resolved>` into\nthe worker command. Unknown/missing label → default. Keep it a pure function for testing.\n\n### 3. Spawn refactor — `drain_cycle/worker.py` (new)\nExtract the spawn block (orchestrator.py:143-178) into a `worker.run_issue(...)` that owns\nthe streamed, guarded spawn and returns a result struct\n(`exit_code`, `usage`, `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `peak_context_tokens`, `session_id`,\n`is_error`, `kill_reason`). Implementation:\n- `subprocess.Popen([claude, -p, --verbose, --output-format, stream-json,\n  --model, <m>, --max-budget-usd, <cap>, --dangerously-skip-permissions, <prompt>],\n  cwd=worktree, stdout=PIPE, stderr=STDOUT, text=True, bufsize=1, start_new_session=True)`.\n  `start_new_session=True` makes the child a process-group leader so we can reap\n  grandchildren.\n- Read `p.stdout` line by line; parse each JSON event. On each event: accumulate cumulative\n  usage + cost + turns, track peak context, update the live marker (step 5), emit a compact\n  progress line to stderr, and check guardrails (step 4).\n- A `threading.Timer` (per-issue time cap) + cycle-deadline check kills the **process\n  group** (`os.killpg(os.getpgid(p.pid), SIGTERM)` then `SIGKILL`) so a quiet long turn\n  still trips the time guard. Cancel the timer on normal completion.\n- Capture the terminal `result` event for the run-log entry.\n- `--max-budget-usd` is the native belt; the orchestrator-side cumulative-token + time caps\n  are the suspenders.\n\n### 4. Guardrails — `drain_cycle/limits.py` (new)\nThresholds with baked-in defaults, overridable by an optional `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`\nand/or CLI flags. Defaults (evidence-grounded, tunable — first real run will calibrate):\n- Per-issue: **8M cumulative tokens** · **20 min** · **$15** (`--max-budget-usd`).\n- Cycle-wide: **30M cumulative tokens** · **90 min** · **$60**.\nOn breach: kill the worker's process group, record a halt entry whose `halt_reason` names\nthe breached limit and the value at kill time, and stop the cycle (same exit-1 + revert\ncontract as existing halts). Each guardrail is independently on/off-able.\n\n### 5. Live observability — `drain_cycle/progress.py` + `status.py` (new)\n- `progress.py`: write/read/clear `~/.drain-cycle/active.json` (one level **above**\n  `runs/` so `grade.py`'s `runs/*.json` glob never sees it). Marker carries:\n  `pid`, `cycle_id`, `run_log_path`, `issue{identifier,title,repo,worktree_path}`,\n  `model`, `started_at`, `index`, `total`, and a live `progress` block\n  (`turns`, `cumulative_tokens`, `peak_context_tokens`, `cost_usd`, `elapsed_seconds`,\n  `last_event_at`). Updated on every stream event; cleared in a `finally` covering all exit\n  paths. `pid` lets `status` detect a stale marker (dead process).\n- `status.py` (`drain-cycle status`): read the marker and print a human view —\n  which issue, model, `[index/total]`, elapsed, turns, **tokens (cumulative + peak) and\n  cost shown together**, against the configured caps (e.g. `12.4M / 8M ⚠`). Live marker →\n  live view; dead pid → stale view; no marker → point at `grade`/run logs.\n- Orchestrator emits a compact per-event stderr line for terminal-watching, e.g.\n  `ABA-205 · turn 42 · 8.1M tok (peak 180k) · $12.30 · 14m`.\n\n### 6. Logging — extend `runlog.py` (additive, grade-safe)\n`grade.py` reads only `cycle_id` + `entries[].final_linear_state`, so new keys are\nbackward-compatible. Add to each entry: `model`, `usage` (input/output/cache_create/\ncache_read/cumulative + peak_context), `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `session_id`, `is_error`,\nexplicit `duration_seconds`. Add top-level aggregates: `cycle_cost_usd`,\n`cycle_tokens_cumulative`. Halt entries from a guardrail breach carry the breach detail in\n`halt_reason`. Update the module docstring schema block.\n\n### 7. Plugin / entire.io investigation (observability-first)\n- The spawn inherits the full parent env and runs in a worktree of the *target* repo;\n  `entire` is a standalone binary (`~/.local/bin/entire`), not in user `enabledPlugins` and\n  not a user hook — so headless `-p` never triggered its checkpointing. Plugins enabled at\n  user level *should* load under `-p` (only `--bare` skips plugin sync), so the divergence\n  needs runtime confirmation, not a guess.\n- Add an opt-in `--debug-file <path>` capture on the worker spawn (written next to the run\n  log) so the next run records exactly which plugins/MCP/hooks initialised.\n- Add a documented one-shot reproduction step (run `claude -p` headless in a worktree, diff\n  loaded plugins vs interactive) to `docs/design-decisions.md`, and a decision entry naming\n  the headless-vs-interactive divergence as a known constraint with the open question. No\n  code fix to plugin loading this round.\n\n### 8. Docs\n- `README.md`: document `drain-cycle status`, the `model:` label, the guardrail limits +\n  `limits.yml`, and the new run-log fields.\n- `docs/design-decisions.md`: entries for stream-json+Popen+process-group kill, the\n  token/cost/time guardrails and defaults, default-Sonnet + `model:` override, the\n  active-marker location (above `runs/`), and the plugin-divergence open question.\n\n## Files\n\n- **New:** `drain_cycle/worker.py`, `drain_cycle/progress.py`, `drain_cycle/status.py`,\n  `drain_cycle/limits.py`, plus a `model:`-label resolver (small fn, in `repos.py` next to\n  the `repo:` logic or its own `model.py`).\n- **Edit:** `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` (call `worker.run_issue`, index/total enumerate,\n  marker write/clear, cycle-deadline + cycle-token aggregate, guardrail-breach halt path),\n  `drain_cycle/runlog.py` (extra fields + aggregates + docstring), `drain_cycle/cli.py`\n  (`status` dispatch + usage), `drain_cycle/prompt.py` (only if model/guardrail context\n  needs surfacing to the worker), `README.md`, `docs/design-decisions.md`.\n- **Tests:** `tests/test_worker.py` (stream parse, guardrail kill via fake claude script\n  emitting stream-json, process-group kill), `tests/test_progress.py`,\n  `tests/test_status.py`, `tests/test_limits.py`, `model:`-label resolution test, run-log\n  new-field test, orchestrator marker-lifecycle + guardrail-halt test, `status` CLI\n  dispatch test. ## Approved Plan (edited by user):
# drain-cycle: diagnose quota burn + add token/time guardrails & live observability

## Context

The last unattended drain (`cycle 3fb7ba8a`, run `20260523T0738`) drained 5 issues in
77 minutes and consumed the operator's entire token quota. Manual work on the same kind
of issue costs ~10 min and a fraction of the tokens. The operator wants to (1) diagnose
what went wrong, (2) be able to monitor a live run (elapsed, progress, tokens, cost),
(3) have hard guardrails on time **and** tokens that kill the worker on breach, and
(4) have all of it written into the run log. A secondary observation — some normal
plugins (e.g. entire.io) didn't load in the headless workers — hints the spawn
environment diverges from an interactive session in ways that weren't intended.

## Diagnosis (evidence)

Token usage reconstructed from the worker session transcripts
(`~/.claude/projects/*worktrees*ABA-*/*.jsonl`), all on **`claude-opus-4-7`**:

| Issue   | Turns | Cache-read | Cache-create | Output | Wall-clock | Outcome |
|---------|------:|-----------:|-------------:|-------:|-----------:|---------|
| ABA-204 |   113 |     16.5M  |       0.99M  |  270K  |     18m    | Done    |
| ABA-205 |   234 |   **43.1M**|       1.23M  |  273K  |     23m    | Done    |
| ABA-134 |   192 |     34.1M  |       1.33M  |  341K  |     21m    | Done    |
| ABA-188 |    49 |      2.7M  |       0.50M  |   36K  |      3m    | Done    |
| ABA-133 |    98 |     11.3M  |       0.58M  |  141K  |     11m    | halt    |
| **Σ**   |  686  | **~108M**  |     ~4.6M    | ~1.06M |     77m    |         |

Root causes, in order of impact:

1. **Cumulative tokens, driven by turn count.** Quota burn = tokens summed across *every*
   turn. Each `claude -p` turn re-reads the whole accumulated context (cache-read), so an
   issue taking 234 turns reads ~180-200k tokens × 234 ≈ 43M. The healthy cheap issue
   (ABA-188) took 49 turns / 3.3M cumulative; the runaways took 100-234 turns.
2. **Opus on every worker.** `claude-opus-4-7` is pinned in user config and inherited by
   every spawn. Opus cache-read/output is ~5× Sonnet — the single biggest cost multiplier.
3. **Heavy per-issue workload.** The prompt (`prompt.py`) tells every worker to run the
   full `/code-review-and-quality` multi-agent skill + a fix loop + commit + push + Linear
   calls — far more turns than a manual edit.
4. **Zero observability.** The run log (`runlog.py`) records only timestamps and exit code
   — no tokens, cost, turns, or model. The operator was flying blind (cannot tell a
   working run from a runaway).
5. **No token/cost/turn guardrail.** Only a 3600s/issue wall-clock timeout exists; nothing
   caps tokens, cost, turns, or total cycle spend. `claude` *does* support
   `--max-budget-usd` (v2.1.150) — unused.
6. **Timeout kills only the direct child.** `subprocess.run(timeout=)` with no
   `start_new_session` leaves grandchildren (MCP servers, sub-agents) orphaned and still
   consuming on a timeout.

**Metric reconciliation (important for threshold calibration).** entire.io reports the
operator's manual sessions at ~66.8k tokens avg (~30k Sonnet, ~100k Opus). That figure is
**peak context occupancy** (largest single turn), not cumulative. A manual issue ≈ one
context fill; an autonomous worker = N turns × context. So the guardrail and display must
show **both**: peak-context (comparable to entire's number) *and* cumulative tokens (the
quota-relevant number), alongside cost and turn count.

## Decisions (from operator)

- **Monitoring:** stream-json — parse `claude`'s event stream live; show progress and log
  final usage.
- **Worker model:** default `claude-sonnet-4-6`; a `model:` Linear label on the issue
  overrides (mirrors the existing `repo:` label mechanism). Ticket wins when set.
- **Guardrails:** kill on time **and** tokens; show cost + tokens together in context.
  Subscription user → token count is primary, cost shown alongside.
- **Plugin issue:** observability-first — capture what loads, add a reproduction step,
  document the divergence; do **not** blind-fix the root cause this round.

## Approach

### 1. Verification spike (gate — do first)
Run once, read-only, to ground the parser in real output (no assuming the schema):
```
claude -p --verbose --output-format stream-json --max-budget-usd 0.50 "say hi"
```
Capture the exact event shapes: the `system`/`init` event, per-turn `assistant` events
(`message.usage`: `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`,
`cache_read_input_tokens`), and the terminal `result` event (`total_cost_usd`, `usage`,
`num_turns`, `duration_ms`, `session_id`, `is_error`, `subtype`). Confirm `--verbose` is
required with `stream-json` in `-p`. **Gate:** only build the parser against confirmed
fields; flag any field that isn't present.

### 2. Worker model resolution — `model:` label
New small resolver mirroring `repos.py`'s `repo:`-label extraction: read a `model:<alias>`
label off `issue["labels"]`, default `claude-sonnet-4-6`. Pass `--model <resolved>` into
the worker command. Unknown/missing label → default. Keep it a pure function for testing.

### 3. Spawn refactor — `drain_cycle/worker.py` (new)
Extract the spawn block (orchestrator.py:143-178) into a `worker.run_issue(...)` that owns
the streamed, guarded spawn and returns a result struct
(`exit_code`, `usage`, `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `peak_context_tokens`, `session_id`,
`is_error`, `kill_reason`). Implementation:
- `subprocess.Popen([claude, -p, --verbose, --output-format, stream-json,
  --model, <m>, --max-budget-usd, <cap>, --dangerously-skip-permissions, <prompt>],
  cwd=worktree, stdout=PIPE, stderr=STDOUT, text=True, bufsize=1, start_new_session=True)`.
  `start_new_session=True` makes the child a process-group leader so we can reap
  grandchildren.
- Read `p.stdout` line by line; parse each JSON event. On each event: accumulate cumulative
  usage + cost + turns, track peak context, update the live marker (step 5), emit a compact
  progress line to stderr, and check guardrails (step 4).
- A `threading.Timer` (per-issue time cap) + cycle-deadline check kills the **process
  group** (`os.killpg(os.getpgid(p.pid), SIGTERM)` then `SIGKILL`) so a quiet long turn
  still trips the time guard. Cancel the timer on normal completion.
- Capture the terminal `result` event for the run-log entry.
- `--max-budget-usd` is the native belt; the orchestrator-side cumulative-token + time caps
  are the suspenders.

### 4. Guardrails — `drain_cycle/limits.py` (new)
Thresholds with baked-in defaults, overridable by an optional `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`
and/or CLI flags. Defaults (evidence-grounded, tunable — first real run will calibrate):
- Per-issue: **8M cumulative tokens** · **20 min** · **$15** (`--max-budget-usd`).
- Cycle-wide: **30M cumulative tokens** · **90 min** · **$60**.
On breach: kill the worker's process group, record a halt entry whose `halt_reason` names
the breached limit and the value at kill time, and stop the cycle (same exit-1 + revert
contract as existing halts). Each guardrail is independently on/off-able.

### 5. Live observability — `drain_cycle/progress.py` + `status.py` (new)
- `progress.py`: write/read/clear `~/.drain-cycle/active.json` (one level **above**
  `runs/` so `grade.py`'s `runs/*.json` glob never sees it). Marker carries:
  `pid`, `cycle_id`, `run_log_path`, `issue{identifier,title,repo,worktree_path}`,
  `model`, `started_at`, `index`, `total`, and a live `progress` block
  (`turns`, `cumulative_tokens`, `peak_context_tokens`, `cost_usd`, `elapsed_seconds`,
  `last_event_at`). Updated on every stream event; cleared in a `finally` covering all exit
  paths. `pid` lets `status` detect a stale marker (dead process).
- `status.py` (`drain-cycle status`): read the marker and print a human view —
  which issue, model, `[index/total]`, elapsed, turns, **tokens (cumulative + peak) and
  cost shown together**, against the configured caps (e.g. `12.4M / 8M ⚠`). Live marker →
  live view; dead pid → stale view; no marker → point at `grade`/run logs.
- Orchestrator emits a compact per-event stderr line for terminal-watching, e.g.
  `ABA-205 · turn 42 · 8.1M tok (peak 180k) · $12.30 · 14m`.

### 6. Logging — extend `runlog.py` (additive, grade-safe)
`grade.py` reads only `cycle_id` + `entries[].final_linear_state`, so new keys are
backward-compatible. Add to each entry: `model`, `usage` (input/output/cache_create/
cache_read/cumulative + peak_context), `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `session_id`, `is_error`,
explicit `duration_seconds`. Add top-level aggregates: `cycle_cost_usd`,
`cycle_tokens_cumulative`. Halt entries from a guardrail breach carry the breach detail in
`halt_reason`. Update the module docstring schema block.

### 7. Plugin / entire.io investigation (observability-first)
- The spawn inherits the full parent env and runs in a worktree of the *target* repo;
  `entire` is a standalone binary (`~/.local/bin/entire`), not in user `enabledPlugins` and
  not a user hook — so headless `-p` never triggered its checkpointing. Plugins enabled at
  user level *should* load under `-p` (only `--bare` skips plugin sync), so the divergence
  needs runtime confirmation, not a guess.
- Add an opt-in `--debug-file <path>` capture on the worker spawn (written next to the run
  log) so the next run records exactly which plugins/MCP/hooks initialised.
- Add a documented one-shot reproduction step (run `claude -p` headless in a worktree, diff
  loaded plugins vs interactive) to `docs/design-decisions.md`, and a decision entry naming
  the headless-vs-interactive divergence as a known constraint with the open question. No
  code fix to plugin loading this round.

### 8. Docs
- `README.md`: document `drain-cycle status`, the `model:` label, the guardrail limits +
  `limits.yml`, and the new run-log fields.
- `docs/design-decisions.md`: entries for stream-json+Popen+process-group kill, the
  token/cost/time guardrails and defaults, default-Sonnet + `model:` override, the
  active-marker location (above `runs/`), and the plugin-divergence open question.

## Files

- **New:** `drain_cycle/worker.py`, `drain_cycle/progress.py`, `drain_cycle/status.py`,
  `drain_cycle/limits.py`, plus a `model:`-label resolver (small fn, in `repos.py` next to
  the `repo:` logic or its own `model.py`).
- **Edit:** `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` (call `worker.run_issue`, index/total enumerate,
  marker write/clear, cycle-deadline + cycle-token aggregate, guardrail-breach halt path),
  `drain_cycle/runlog.py` (extra fields + aggregates + docstring), `drain_cycle/cli.py`
  (`status` dispatch + usage), `drain_cycle/prompt.py` (only if model/guardrail context
  needs surfacing to the worker), `README.md`, `docs/design-decisions.md`.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_worker.py` (stream parse, guardrail kill via fake claude script
  emitting stream-json, process-group kill), `tests/test_progress.py`,
  `tests/test_status.py`, `tests/test_limits.py`, `model:`-label resolution test, run-log
  new-field test, orchestrator marker-lifecycle + guardrail-halt test, `status` CLI
  dispatch test. Reuse existing test conventions (`_issue(...)` helper, fake-claude shell
  script via `_CLAUDE_CMD` monkeypatch, `monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", tmp_path)`).

## Verification

1. **Spike gate:** event schema confirmed against real `claude -p --output-format
   stream-json` output before the parser is written.
2. **Unit suite green:** `uv run pytest` — including a fake-claude script that emits a
   scripted stream-json sequence and one that overshoots a tiny token cap to prove the
   process-group kill fires and records the right `halt_reason`.
3. **End-to-end (one real issue):** point drain-cycle at a single throwaway issue with a
   deliberately tiny per-issue token cap; confirm (a) `drain-cycle status` shows live
   tokens+cost+turns+elapsed from another terminal, (b) the worker is killed on cap breach
   and its whole process group dies (no orphaned `claude`/MCP procs — check `pgrep -fl
   claude`), (c) the run-log entry carries model/usage/cost/turns/session_id and the breach
   reason, (d) the `--debug-file` capture shows which plugins loaded headless.
4. **Calibration:** with real numbers in hand, confirm the default caps catch a runaway
   like ABA-205 while letting a healthy issue like ABA-188 finish; adjust `limits.yml`.

## Linear — ticket breakdown

This is initiative-sized, not one issue. Split into **5 vertical-slice issues** under the
existing **Autonomous cycle drain** project (no new project — issues live under the
initiative that already owns this work). Each is independently shippable and verifiable,
and each carries a recommended worker model per the new default-Sonnet + `model:`-label
convention this work introduces. The first implementation step is creating these issues in
Linear (and **folding ABA-234** "status command + streaming" into T-D — close/repurpose it).

| # | Title (problem-framed) | Scope | Depends on | Model | Why this model |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T-A** | Worker model is unconfigurable — default to Sonnet, let the ticket override | `model:`-label resolver mirroring `repo:`; default `claude-sonnet-4-6`; pass `--model` to the worker | — | **Sonnet** | Small, mirrors an existing pattern |
| **T-B** | Run logs are token-blind — stream the worker and record usage | Spawn refactor to `Popen` + `--verbose --output-format stream-json`; capture `result` event (usage/cost/turns/session_id/peak-context); `start_new_session` + process-group kill on the existing time cap; extend run-log schema to persist it | — | **Opus** | Concurrency, signal handling, stream parsing — the trickiest slice |
| **T-C** | No token/time/cost guardrail — kill a worker that breaches limits | `limits.py` (+ optional `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`); `--max-budget-usd` belt + orchestrator cumulative-token & cycle-wide suspenders; breach → process-group kill → recorded halt | T-B | **Opus** | Kill semantics + edge cases |
| **T-D** | A live run is uninspectable — `active.json` marker + `drain-cycle status` | `progress.py` + `status.py` + compact stderr progress line; shows tokens (cumulative + peak) + cost + turns + elapsed vs caps. **Absorbs ABA-234.** | T-B | **Sonnet** | Moderate, well-specified |
| **T-E** | Plugins (entire.io) don't load headless — capture and document the divergence | Opt-in `--debug-file` on the spawn; documented reproduction step; design-decision entry. No blind fix. | T-B | **Opus** | Investigation/judgment-heavy, low code |

**Sequencing:** T-A and T-B first (T-A independent; T-B foundational). T-C, T-D, T-E all
build on T-B and can be worked in any order after it. Critical path: T-B → {T-C, T-D, T-E}.

The verification spike (Approach §1) is a prerequisite gate for **T-B** — confirm the
stream-json event schema against real `claude` output before writing the parser.

**Per-issue workflow** (every ticket): on start → In Progress + assign to the current
cycle if shipping this cycle. On completion → `/code-review-and-quality` → fix
Critical/Required → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done. Code comments
must not carry ticket IDs / SHAs / fix-history narration (repo `AGENTS.md` rule); no
`Co-Authored-By` trailers.

> AGENT

The spike **confirms the schema** — no guessing needed for the parser:

- **`assistant` events** carry `message.usage` per turn: `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`.
- **Terminal `result` event** (authoritative): `total_cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `session_id`, `is_error`, `usage`, and `modelUsage[model].contextWindow`.
- Bonus: a **`rate_limit_event`** type exists (worth logging for a subscription user), and **hooks DO fire in `-p`** — the entire.io SessionStart hook fired here (`"Entire CLI will link this conversation to your next commit"`). The difference vs the failed workers is the **worktree cwd** — a strong lead for T-E.

Also telling: "say hi" cost **$0.16 and 43k cache-creation tokens** — that's the global CLAUDE.md rule-import chain loaded fresh every session. Confirms the fixed-context tax.

Parser design locked: sum per-turn usage live for the token guardrail; take the `result` event as authoritative totals (fall back to accumulated if killed mid-stream). Now let me create the Linear issues.

{"id":"ABA-234","title":"drain-cycle: live in-flight progress (`status` command + worker streaming)","description":"## Problem\n\nA running `drain-cycle` is hard to observe. Mid-issue there is almost no signal:\n\n* The spawned `claude -p` worker runs without `--verbose`/`--output-format`, so in `-p` mode it emits only its *final* message — an attached terminal shows nothing for the whole issue, then a blob.\n* The orchestrator prints only three sparse stderr lines (`picked`, `done`, `Halt:`).\n* The run-log gains an entry only when an issue *finishes*, so mid-issue it's `entries: []` — invisible.\n* There is no way to inspect from a second terminal or when the run was launched detached.\n\nNet effect: the operator cannot tell a working run from a hung one without manually walking the process tree and the target-repo worktree.\n\n## Changes\n\n1. **Worker streaming** — add `--verbose` to the spawned `claude -p` command so its turn-by-turn activity streams to the launching terminal (the orchestrator's `subprocess.run` already inherits stdout/stderr, so no capture plumbing).\n2. **Active-issue marker** (`drain_cycle/progress.py`) — the orchestrator writes `~/.drain-cycle/active.json` just before each spawn (cycle id, run-log path, issue identifier/title/repo/worktree, started_at, index, total, orchestrator pid) and clears it as soon as the worker returns (try/finally, covers done/halt/timeout). Marker lives **above** `runs/` so `grade`'s `runs/*.json` glob never mistakes it for a run-log file.\n3. `drain-cycle status` (`drain_cycle/status.py`) — reads the marker from any terminal and reports the in-flight issue: which one, repo, elapsed, worktree file changes, and done/remaining this run. Detects a stale marker (orchestrator pid gone → crashed/killed) and a no-marker state (points at `grade`).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n* `drain-cycle status` during a run shows the current issue, its repo, elapsed time, `git status --short` of the worktree, and done/remaining counts.\n* `drain-cycle status` with no run prints \"No active drain-cycle run\" and exits 0.\n* A stale marker (pid not alive) is reported as such, not as a live run.\n* The marker is written before the worker spawns and removed after it returns on every exit path (done / halt / timeout).\n* `grade` output is unaffected by the marker file (it does not live in `runs/`).\n* Run-log schema unchanged.\n* New tests for `progress`, `status`, the orchestrator marker lifecycle, and the `status` CLI dispatch; full suite green.\n\n## Files\n\n* `drain_cycle/progress.py` (new), `drain_cycle/status.py` (new)\n* `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` (marker write/clear + `--verbose` + index/total)\n* `drain_cycle/cli.py` (`status` dispatch + usage)\n* `tests/` (new tests), `README.md` (usage), `docs/design-decisions.md` (marker-location decision)","priority":{"value":0,"name":"No priority"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-234/drain-cycle-live-in-flight-progress-status-command-worker-streaming","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-234-drain-cycle-live-in-flight-progress-status-command-worker","createdAt":"2026-05-23T08:01:33.626Z","updatedAt":"2026-05-23T12:04:32.651Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Todo","statusType":"unstarted","labels":["repo:drain-cycle"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Autonomous cycle drain - eliminate manual shepherding","projectId":"3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1","cycleId":"3fb7ba8a-ff55-49c0-bf35-b879d3a4b83c"}
```
