```json
{
  "title": "drain-cycle: default workers to Sonnet, let the ticket override via `model:` label",
  "team": "Personal",
  "project": "3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf",
  "cycle": "3fb7ba8a-ff55-49c0-bf35-b879d3a4b83c",
  "labels": [
    "repo:drain-cycle",
    "model:sonnet"
  ],
  "description": "## Problem\n\nEvery spawned worker inherits the operator's globally-pinned `claude-opus-4-7`. In the last drain that put all 5 issues on Opus — the single biggest driver of the ~108M-token quota burn (Opus cache-read/output is ~5× Sonnet). The worker model is not configurable per run or per issue.\n\n## Change\n\nDefault workers to `claude-sonnet-4-6`; allow a per-issue override via a `model:<alias>` Linear label (mirrors the existing `repo:` label mechanism in `repos.py`). Ticket wins when set.\n\n- New resolver (pure function, next to the `repo:` extraction in `repos.py` or a small `model.py`): read a `model:<alias>` label off `issue[\"labels\"]`; default `claude-sonnet-4-6`; unknown/missing → default.\n- Orchestrator passes `--model <resolved>` into the worker command.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- A worker with no `model:` label runs `claude-sonnet-4-6`.\n- A worker on an issue labelled `model:opus` runs the Opus alias.\n- Resolver is unit-tested for: no label, `model:sonnet`, `model:opus`, unknown alias (→ default).\n- Full suite green.\n\n## Recommended worker model\n\n**Sonnet** — small, well-specified change mirroring an existing pattern.\n\n## Files\n\n`drain_cycle/repos.py` (or new `drain_cycle/model.py`), `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`, `tests/`, `README.md` (`model:` label), `docs/design-decisions.md` (default-Sonnet + override decision)."
}
```

id: toolu_017vEQD8WQUJhdFDkuvUJba7
```json
{
  "title": "drain-cycle: run logs are token-blind — stream the worker and record usage",
  "team": "Personal",
  "project": "3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf",
  "cycle": "3fb7ba8a-ff55-49c0-bf35-b879d3a4b83c",
  "labels": [
    "repo:drain-cycle",
    "model:opus"
  ],
  "description": "## Problem\n\nThe run log records only timestamps and exit code — no tokens, cost, turns, or model. After a run that burned ~108M tokens the operator had no on-disk way to see which issue spent what; usage had to be reconstructed from `~/.claude/projects/*.jsonl`. The worker is also spawned with `subprocess.run(timeout=)` and no `start_new_session`, so on a timeout only the direct child is killed and grandchildren (MCP servers, sub-agents) are orphaned and keep consuming.\n\n## Change\n\nRefactor the spawn into a new `drain_cycle/worker.py` (`run_issue(...)`) that streams and records usage:\n\n- Spawn `claude -p --verbose --output-format stream-json --model <m> --dangerously-skip-permissions <prompt>` via `Popen(..., stdout=PIPE, stderr=STDOUT, text=True, bufsize=1, start_new_session=True)`.\n- Parse the event stream line by line. Accumulate per-turn `assistant` `message.usage` (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`); track cumulative tokens and peak-context (max per-turn input+cache_read+cache_create). Capture the terminal `result` event (`total_cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `session_id`, `is_error`, `usage`). Fall back to accumulated totals if killed before a `result` event.\n- `start_new_session=True` + process-group kill (`os.killpg`) on the existing per-issue time cap, so grandchildren are reaped.\n- Extend the run-log schema (additive — `grade.py` reads only `cycle_id` + `final_linear_state`): per-entry `model`, `usage` (input/output/cache_create/cache_read/cumulative + peak_context), `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `session_id`, `is_error`, explicit `duration_seconds`; top-level `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_tokens_cumulative`. Update the docstring schema block.\n\nEvent schema confirmed against real `claude` v2.1.150 stream-json output (verification spike).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- A completed issue's run-log entry carries model, cumulative + peak-context tokens, cost_usd, num_turns, session_id.\n- `grade` output is unchanged by the new fields.\n- A worker that times out has its whole process group killed (no orphaned `claude`/MCP procs — `pgrep -fl claude` clean afterwards).\n- Tests use a fake-claude script emitting a scripted stream-json sequence (incl. a `result` event) and a hanging script for the timeout/process-group-kill path; full suite green.\n\n## Recommended worker model\n\n**Opus** — concurrency, signal handling, and stream parsing; the trickiest slice.\n\n## Files\n\n`drain_cycle/worker.py` (new), `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` (call `worker.run_issue`), `drain_cycle/runlog.py` (schema + aggregates + docstring), `tests/test_worker.py` + run-log tests, `docs/design-decisions.md` (stream-json + Popen + process-group-kill decision).\n\nFoundational: blocks the guardrails, live-status, and plugin-debug slices."
}
```

id: toolu_01X9mkVEpjfVgw8skvVd7MB5
```json
{
  "title": "drain-cycle: no token/time/cost guardrail — kill a worker that breaches limits",
  "team": "Personal",
  "project": "3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf",
  "cycle": "3fb7ba8a-ff55-49c0-bf35-b879d3a4b83c",
  "labels": [
    "repo:drain-cycle"
  ],
  "blockedBy": [
    "ABA-236"
  ],
  "description": "## Problem\n\nThe only limit on a worker is a 3600s/issue wall-clock timeout. Nothing caps tokens, cost, or total cycle spend. One issue (ABA-205) ran 234 turns / 43M tokens / 23 min unchecked, and the cycle had no ceiling at all — five such issues drained the whole quota with no circuit-breaker.\n\n## Change\n\nAdd `drain_cycle/limits.py` with thresholds (baked-in defaults, overridable by optional `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml` and/or CLI flags), and enforce them in the streamed worker:\n\n- **Native belt:** pass `--max-budget-usd <per-issue $cap>` so `claude` self-terminates on cost.\n- **Orchestrator suspenders:** from the live event stream, kill the worker's process group when per-issue cumulative tokens or elapsed time breaches; track cycle-wide cumulative tokens / cost / wall-clock and stop the cycle when the cycle cap breaches.\n- On breach: process-group kill, then a recorded halt entry whose `halt_reason` names the breached limit and its value at kill time (same exit-1 + revert contract as existing halts).\n- Each guardrail independently on/off-able.\n\nDefaults (tunable; calibrate after first real run): per-issue 8M tokens · 20 min · $15; cycle 30M tokens · 90 min · $60. Token count is the primary guardrail (subscription user); cost shown alongside.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- A worker exceeding the per-issue token cap is killed (whole process group) and produces a halt entry naming the breached limit + value.\n- The cycle stops when a cycle-wide cap is breached.\n- `--max-budget-usd` is passed to the worker.\n- Limits load from defaults, with `limits.yml` override honoured.\n- Tests cover each breach path with a fake-claude script that overshoots a tiny cap; full suite green.\n\n## Recommended worker model\n\n**Opus** — kill semantics and breach edge cases.\n\n## Files\n\n`drain_cycle/limits.py` (new), `drain_cycle/worker.py` (+ orchestrator cycle-wide aggregation), `tests/test_limits.py` + worker breach tests, `README.md` (limits + `limits.yml`), `docs/design-decisions.md` (guardrails + defaults decision).\n\nDepends on the streamed worker (ABA-236)."
}
```

```json
{
  "title": "drain-cycle: plugins (entire.io) don't load headless — capture and document the divergence",
  "team": "Personal",
  "project": "3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf",
  "cycle": "3fb7ba8a-ff55-49c0-bf35-b879d3a4b83c",
  "labels": [
    "repo:drain-cycle"
  ],
  "blockedBy": [
    "ABA-236"
  ],
  "description": "## Problem\n\nThe operator noticed some normal plugins (e.g. entire.io's checkpointing) didn't take effect during the headless drain, hinting the spawn environment diverges from an interactive session more than intended. A verification spike showed hooks *do* fire under `claude -p` (entire's SessionStart hook fired when run from a normal repo root) — so the likely difference is the worker's **worktree cwd** (`.worktrees/<id>` of the target repo) and/or where entire's hook is registered. Root cause needs runtime confirmation, not a guess.\n\n## Change (observability-first — no blind fix)\n\n- Add an opt-in `--debug-file <path>` to the worker spawn, written next to the run log, so the next run records exactly which plugins / MCP servers / hooks initialised.\n- Add a documented one-shot reproduction step to `docs/design-decisions.md`: run `claude -p` headless in a worktree vs a repo root and diff the loaded plugins/hooks.\n- Add a design-decision entry naming the headless-vs-interactive divergence as a known constraint, with the open question and the spike evidence (hooks fire under `-p`; the suspect is worktree cwd).\n\nNo code change to plugin/hook loading this round.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- `--debug-file` capture is wired into the spawn (opt-in) and lands beside the run log.\n- `docs/design-decisions.md` has the reproduction step + divergence decision entry.\n- A reproduction run confirms (or refutes) the worktree-cwd hypothesis and the finding is recorded.\n\n## Recommended worker model\n\n**Opus** — investigation/judgment-heavy, low code.\n\n## Files\n\n`drain_cycle/worker.py` (opt-in `--debug-file`), `docs/design-decisions.md` (reproduction + decision), `README.md` (debug capture note).\n\nDepends on the streamed worker (ABA-236)."
}
```

```
{"id":"ABA-238","title":"drain-cycle: plugins (entire.io) don't load headless — capture and document the divergence","description":"## Problem\n\nThe operator noticed some normal plugins (e.g. [entire.io](<http://entire.io>)'s checkpointing) didn't take effect during the headless drain, hinting the spawn environment diverges from an interactive session more than intended. A verification spike showed hooks *do* fire under `claude -p` (entire's SessionStart hook fired when run from a normal repo root) — so the likely difference is the worker's **worktree cwd** (`.worktrees/<id>` of the target repo) and/or where entire's hook is registered. Root cause needs runtime confirmation, not a guess.\n\n## Change (observability-first — no blind fix)\n\n* Add an opt-in `--debug-file <path>` to the worker spawn, written next to the run log, so the next run records exactly which plugins / MCP servers / hooks initialised.\n* Add a documented one-shot reproduction step to `docs/design-decisions.md`: run `claude -p` headless in a worktree vs a repo root and diff the loaded plugins/hooks.\n* Add a design-decision entry naming the headless-vs-interactive divergence as a known constraint, with the open question and the spike evidence (hooks fire under `-p`; the suspect is worktree cwd).\n\nNo code change to plugin/hook loading this round.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n* `--debug-file` capture is wired into the spawn (opt-in) and lands beside the run log.\n* `docs/design-decisions.md` has the reproduction step + divergence decision entry.\n* A reproduction run confirms (or refutes) the worktree-cwd hypothesis and the finding is recorded.\n\n## Recommended worker model\n\n**Opus** — investigation/judgment-heavy, low code.\n\n## Files\n\n`drain_cycle/worker.py` (opt-in `--debug-file`), `docs/design-decisions.md` (reproduction + decision), `README.md` (debug capture note).\n\nDepends on the streamed worker (<issue id=\"eb62c999-a798-4055-9d3c-7993542fbdf8\">ABA-236</issue>).","priority":{"value":0,"name":"No priority"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-238/drain-cycle-plugins-entireio-dont-load-headless-capture-and-document","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-238-drain-cycle-plugins-entireio-dont-load-headless-capture-and","createdAt":"2026-05-23T12:39:13.809Z","updatedAt":"2026-05-23T12:39:14.363Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Todo","statusType":"unstarted","labels":["repo:drain-cycle"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Autonomous cycle drain - eliminate manual shepherding","projectId":"3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1","cycleId":"3fb7ba8a-ff55-49c0-bf35-b879d3a4b83c"}
```

```json
{
  "id": "ABA-234",
  "blockedBy": [
    "ABA-236"
  ],
  "title": "drain-cycle: a live run is uninspectable — `active.json` marker + `status` command",
  "description": "## Problem\n\nA running `drain-cycle` is hard to observe. Mid-issue there is almost no signal: the run-log gains an entry only when an issue *finishes*, the orchestrator prints only three sparse stderr lines, and there's no way to inspect from a second terminal or when the run was launched detached. The operator cannot tell a working run from a hung/runaway one.\n\n## Change\n\nBuilds on the streamed worker (ABA-236), so the live view is **token-aware**:\n\n1. **Active-issue marker** (`drain_cycle/progress.py`) — the orchestrator writes `~/.drain-cycle/active.json` just before each spawn and clears it as soon as the worker returns (try/finally over done/halt/timeout/breach). Lives **above** `runs/` so `grade`'s `runs/*.json` glob never sees it. Carries: `pid`, `cycle_id`, `run_log_path`, `issue{identifier,title,repo,worktree_path}`, `model`, `started_at`, `index`, `total`, and a live `progress` block updated on every stream event (`turns`, `cumulative_tokens`, `peak_context_tokens`, `cost_usd`, `elapsed_seconds`, `last_event_at`).\n2. **`drain-cycle status`** (`drain_cycle/status.py`) — reads the marker from any terminal: which issue, repo, model, `[index/total]`, elapsed, turns, and **tokens (cumulative + peak) with cost shown together** against the configured caps (e.g. `12.4M / 8M ⚠`). Detects a stale marker (pid gone → crashed/killed) and a no-marker state (points at `grade`).\n3. **Compact stderr progress line** per stream event for terminal-watching, e.g. `ABA-205 · turn 42 · 8.1M tok (peak 180k) · $12.30 · 14m`.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- `drain-cycle status` during a run shows the current issue, repo, model, elapsed, turns, cumulative + peak tokens, and cost — vs caps.\n- `drain-cycle status` with no run prints a no-active-run message and exits 0.\n- A stale marker (pid not alive) is reported as such, not as a live run.\n- Marker written before spawn, removed after the worker returns on every exit path.\n- `grade` output is unaffected by the marker file.\n- New tests for `progress`, `status`, the orchestrator marker lifecycle, and the `status` CLI dispatch; full suite green.\n\n## Recommended worker model\n\n**Sonnet** — moderate, well-specified.\n\n## Files\n\n`drain_cycle/progress.py` (new), `drain_cycle/status.py` (new), `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` (marker write/clear + index/total), `drain_cycle/cli.py` (`status` dispatch + usage), `tests/`, `README.md`, `docs/design-decisions.md` (marker-location decision).\n\nDepends on the streamed worker (ABA-236) for the live token feed."
}
```

```
1	"""Per-issue target-repo resolution.
2	
3	The orchestrator used to be single-repo by construction: every worktree
4	landed under ``Path.cwd()``. Cycles in this workspace span multiple repos
5	by design — ``linear-workflow.md`` makes "Affected repos" part of the
6	six initiative-readiness fields, and the Ops slot deliberately holds
7	cross-repo maintenance issues — so each issue now carries a
8	``repo:<name>`` label and ``~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`` maps the name to
9	an absolute path on disk.
10	
11	Two distinct error types so the two halt paths can branch cleanly:
12	
13	* ``RepoConfigError`` — startup-time problems (missing file, malformed
14	  YAML, empty, wrong top-level shape). The CLI catches it before any
15	  Linear traffic, prints to stderr, exits 1, and does **not** write a
16	  run-log entry — there is no cycle to log against yet.
17	* ``RepoResolutionError`` — per-issue problems (no ``repo:`` label,
18	  multiple ``repo:`` labels, name not in config, resolved path missing).
19	  The orchestrator catches it as a pre-spawn halt: write a run-log
20	  entry, print the matching ``Halt:`` line, exit 1. No revert is
21	  attempted because no Linear state was moved yet.
22	"""
23	from __future__ import annotations
24	
25	from dataclasses import dataclass
26	from pathlib import Path
27	from typing import Any
28	
29	import yaml
30	
31	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml"
32	_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX = "repo:"
33	
34	_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT = (
35	    "create it mapping each repo: label to an absolute path, e.g.:\n\n"
36	    "  repos:\n"
37	    "    drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle\n"
38	    "    pde-skills:  /Users/you/src/pde-skills\n\n"
39	    "see docs/repos.example.yml for a template."
40	)
41	
42	
43	class RepoConfigError(RuntimeError):
44	    """``repos.yml`` is missing or malformed."""
45	
46	
47	class RepoResolutionError(RuntimeError):
48	    """An issue cannot be mapped to a target repo."""
49	
50	
51	def default_config_path() -> Path:
52	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
53	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "repos.yml"
54	
55	
56	@dataclass(frozen=True)
57	class Repos:
58	    """Loaded ``repos.yml`` mapping (``name`` → absolute ``Path``)."""
59	
60	    mapping: dict[str, Path]
61	
62	    def resolve(self, issue: dict[str, Any]) -> Path:
63	        labels = [
64	            label
65	            for label in issue.get("labels", [])
66	            if label.startswith(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
67	        ]
68	        if not labels:
69	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"no {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} label on issue")
70	        if len(labels) > 1:
71	            names = ", ".join(sorted(label.removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX) for label in labels))
72	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"multiple {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} labels: {names}")
73	        name = labels[0].removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
74	        try:
75	            path = self.mapping[name]
76	        except KeyError:
77	            raise RepoResolutionError(
78	                f'repo "{name}" not in {_CONFIG_DISPLAY}'
79	            ) from None
80	        if not path.exists():
81	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"resolved path {path} does not exist")
82	        return path
83	
84	
85	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Repos:
86	    """Read ``repos.yml`` and return a validated ``Repos``.
87	
88	    Tilde-prefixed paths inside the file are expanded against ``$HOME``.
89	    Every failure mode raises ``RepoConfigError`` with a message that
90	    names the on-disk problem so the operator can fix it without
91	    re-reading source.
92	    """
93	    if path is None:
94	        path = default_config_path()
95	    if not path.exists():
96	        raise RepoConfigError(
97	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} not found at {path}\n{_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT}"
98	        )
99	    try:
100	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
101	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
102	        raise RepoConfigError(
103	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}"
104	        ) from None
105	    if data is None:
106	        raise RepoConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is empty")
107	    if not isinstance(data, dict) or "repos" not in data:
108	        raise RepoConfigError(
109	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must have a top-level 'repos:' key"
110	        )
111	    block = data["repos"]
112	    if not isinstance(block, dict) or not block:
113	        raise RepoConfigError(
114	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'repos:' must be a non-empty mapping"
115	        )
116	    mapping: dict[str, Path] = {}
117	    for name, value in block.items():
118	        if not isinstance(value, str):
119	            raise RepoConfigError(
120	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: path must be a string"
121	            )
122	        mapping[str(name)] = Path(value).expanduser()
123	    return Repos(mapping=mapping)
124	
```

```
{"id":"ABA-235","title":"drain-cycle: default workers to Sonnet, let the ticket override via `model:` label","description":"## Problem\n\nEvery spawned worker inherits the operator's globally-pinned `claude-opus-4-7`. In the last drain that put all 5 issues on Opus — the single biggest driver of the \\~108M-token quota burn (Opus cache-read/output is \\~5× Sonnet). The worker model is not configurable per run or per issue.\n\n## Change\n\nDefault workers to `claude-sonnet-4-6`; allow a per-issue override via a `model:<alias>` Linear label (mirrors the existing `repo:` label mechanism in `repos.py`). Ticket wins when set.\n\n* New resolver (pure function, next to the `repo:` extraction in `repos.py` or a small `model.py`): read a `model:<alias>` label off `issue[\"labels\"]`; default `claude-sonnet-4-6`; unknown/missing → default.\n* Orchestrator passes `--model <resolved>` into the worker command.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n* A worker with no `model:` label runs `claude-sonnet-4-6`.\n* A worker on an issue labelled `model:opus` runs the Opus alias.\n* Resolver is unit-tested for: no label, `model:sonnet`, `model:opus`, unknown alias (→ default).\n* Full suite green.\n\n## Recommended worker model\n\n**Sonnet** — small, well-specified change mirroring an existing pattern.\n\n## Files\n\n`drain_cycle/repos.py` (or new `drain_cycle/model.py`), `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`, `tests/`, `README.md` (`model:` label), `docs/design-decisions.md` (default-Sonnet + override decision).","priority":{"value":0,"name":"No priority"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-235/drain-cycle-default-workers-to-sonnet-let-the-ticket-override-via","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-235-drain-cycle-default-workers-to-sonnet-let-the-ticket","createdAt":"2026-05-23T12:38:25.881Z","updatedAt":"2026-05-23T12:40:48.898Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":"2026-05-23T12:40:48.881Z","canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"In Progress","statusType":"started","labels":["repo:drain-cycle"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Autonomous cycle drain - eliminate manual shepherding","projectId":"3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1","cycleId":"3fb7ba8a-ff55-49c0-bf35-b879d3a4b83c"}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/model.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Per-issue worker-model resolution.\n\nWorkers default to a cost-efficient model. A spawned ``claude -p`` session\ninherits whatever model the operator has globally pinned, which can be the\nmost expensive one — so unattended cycles default to Sonnet here and let an\nindividual issue opt up (or down) via a ``model:<alias>`` Linear label,\nmirroring the ``repo:<name>`` mechanism in ``repos.py``.\n\nResolution is deliberately lenient and never raises: a worker always has a\nsafe model to run, so any ambiguity falls back to the default rather than\nhalting an unattended cycle. The model actually used is recorded in the run\nlog, so a mis-labelled issue surfaces after the fact instead of stalling.\n\n* No ``model:`` label → ``DEFAULT_MODEL``.\n* Exactly one label → its value, mapped through ``_ALIASES`` if known,\n  otherwise passed through verbatim (``claude --model`` validates it).\n* More than one label → ``DEFAULT_MODEL`` (ambiguous intent; the cheap,\n  safe default wins).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import Any\n\n_MODEL_LABEL_PREFIX = \"model:\"\n\nDEFAULT_MODEL = \"claude-sonnet-4-6\"\n\n_ALIASES = {\n    \"sonnet\": \"claude-sonnet-4-6\",\n    \"opus\": \"claude-opus-4-7\",\n    \"haiku\": \"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001\",\n}\n\n\ndef resolve(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the model the worker for ``issue`` should run on.\"\"\"\n    values = [\n        label.removeprefix(_MODEL_LABEL_PREFIX)\n        for label in issue.get(\"labels\", [])\n        if label.startswith(_MODEL_LABEL_PREFIX)\n    ]\n    if len(values) != 1:\n        return DEFAULT_MODEL\n    requested = values[0]\n    return _ALIASES.get(requested, requested)\n"
}
```

74	## Usage
75	
76	```
77	drain-cycle
78	```
79	
80	Run from anywhere — `drain-cycle` resolves each issue's target repo from its `repo:<name>` label. Drains the current cycle's Todo/Backlog issues (in priority order) until either the cycle is empty (exit 0) or an issue halts (exit 1).
81	
82	### What a run looks like
83	
84	```bash
85	$ drain-cycle
86	
87	# drain-cycle reads the current Linear cycle, fetches its Todo/Backlog issues,
88	# and works through them one at a time. Each issue's target repo is resolved
89	# from its `repo:<name>` label against ~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml.
90	
91	drain-cycle: picked ABA-241: Persist halt_reason in run-log entries
92	
93	# ABA-241 is labelled `repo:drain-cycle`, which `repos.yml` maps to
94	# ~/src/drain-cycle. The orchestrator creates a fresh git worktree under
95	# ~/src/drain-cycle/.worktrees/ABA-241/ branched off main, flips the Linear
96	# issue to In Progress, then spawns a `claude -p` session inside the worktree.
97	
98	  (… claude -p session does the work: reads the issue, edits files,
99	       runs tests, commits, pushes, marks the issue Done in Linear …)
100	
101	drain-cycle: ABA-241 done; worktree removed.
102	drain-cycle: picked PDE-12: Add the new triage skill
103	
104	# PDE-12 is labelled `repo:pde-skills`, so this run lands in a worktree
105	# under ~/src/pde-skills/.worktrees/PDE-12/ — different repo, same machinery.
106	
107	  (… second session runs …)
108	
109	drain-cycle: PDE-12 done; worktree removed.
110	drain-cycle: picked ABA-243: Compute cycle_duration_seconds
111	
112	  (… third session runs, but doesn't finish the work …)
113	
114	# The session exited but the Linear issue is still In Progress — meaning the
115	# agent didn't mark it Done. drain-cycle stops the run immediately so you can
116	# investigate. The worktree is left in place; the run log captures the same
117	# halt line you see on screen.
118	
119	Halt: ABA-243 (final state: In Progress) at /Users/you/src/drain-cycle/.worktrees/ABA-243
120	$ echo $?
121	1
122	```
123	
124	After the run, one JSON log per invocation is at `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`. On halt, the worktree at `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/` is preserved for inspection; on success it's removed.
125	
126	## Recommended companion skills
127	
128	`drain-cycle` assumes the spawned `claude -p` sessions have access to whatever skills you've installed globally. The pairing it was designed for:
129	
130	- [**`ababushkin/pde-skills`**](https://github.com/ababushkin/pde-skills) — planning + engineering skill pack. Use it to *shape* the cycle (initiative, KRs, slices) before draining it. `drain-cycle` only multiplies execution; the quality of the cycle is upstream of this tool.
131	- [**`addyosmani/agent-skills`**](https://github.com/addyosmani/agent-skills) — Addy Osmani's pack covers complementary build / test / review skills the spawned sessions lean on.
132	
133	Both are skill packs for Claude Code — install them globally and the spawned `claude -p` sessions will pick them up automatically.
```

20	- **`git` CLI** on `$PATH`.
21	- **`claude` CLI** on `$PATH` — see the [Claude Code install docs](https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/claude-code/setup).
22	- **Linear API key** with read/write on your Personal team. Generate one at <https://linear.app/settings/api>.
23	- **A `repo:<name>` Linear label on every cycle issue.** `drain-cycle` resolves the target repo per issue from this label — an unlabelled issue halts the run before any worktree is created. Create the labels at the team level in Linear (Settings → Labels) using the exact names your `repos.yml` keys use.
24	- **A `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml` config file** mapping each `<name>` to the absolute path of the repo on disk (see Install below).
25	- **Each target repo must gitignore `.worktrees/`.** `drain-cycle` creates `.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/` inside the target repo per spawned session; without an ignore rule the spawned `git add` calls can sweep those paths into commits. A one-line `.gitignore` entry per repo is enough.
26	
27	## Install
28	
29	Install with `uv` so the `drain-cycle` executable lands on `$PATH` in an isolated environment — no venv to activate, runs from any directory:
30	
31	```bash
32	uv tool install .                                          # from a local checkout
33	uv tool install git+https://github.com/ababushkin/drain-cycle  # or straight from the repo
34	```
35	
36	`uv` installs the executable under `~/.local/bin`. If that isn't on your `$PATH`, run `uv tool update-shell` once. The CLI resolves each issue's target repo from its `repo:<name>` label, so the directory you run it from doesn't matter.
37	
38	Then set up `~/.drain-cycle/`, which holds everything the tool reads and writes (config, secret, run logs):
39	
40	```bash
41	mkdir -p ~/.drain-cycle
42	echo 'LINEAR_API_KEY=lin_api_…' > ~/.drain-cycle/.env
43	```
44	
45	The key is read from the first source that defines it: a shell-exported `LINEAR_API_KEY` wins, then `~/.drain-cycle/.env`, then a `.env` at the repo root (a dev-checkout fallback the installed tool never sees). Export it in your shell rc instead of the file if you prefer.
46	
47	Create `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml` mapping each label name to the repo's absolute path on disk:
48	
49	```yaml
50	repos:
51	  drain-cycle:  /Users/you/src/drain-cycle
52	  pde-skills:   /Users/you/src/pde-skills
53	  stock-review: /Users/you/src/stock-review
54	```
55	
56	A `~`-prefixed path inside the file is expanded against `$HOME`. A missing or malformed `repos.yml` halts the CLI before any Linear traffic — exit 1, message on stderr, no run-log entry written. See [`docs/repos.example.yml`](docs/repos.example.yml) for a copyable template.
57	
58	Verify the install from anywhere:
59	
60	```bash
61	cd /tmp && drain-cycle --help     # any invocation that doesn't crash on import works
62	```
63	
64	### Developing on drain-cycle
65	
66	Working on the tool itself? Pin the dev Python and install editable so source edits take effect without reinstalling:
67	
68	```bash
69	mise install                       # picks up mise.toml → Python 3.12
70	uv tool install --editable .       # symlinks the checkout
71	uv tool install --reinstall .      # or rebuild from scratch after a change
72	```
73	
```

```
31	## 4. Run-log is one file per invocation, not one file per cycle
32	
33	Each `drain-cycle` invocation writes its own run-log file at `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`. The per-file schema is unchanged — `{cycle_id, cycle_duration_seconds, entries: [...]}` — and the `cycle_id` carried inside each file is how downstream readers group runs of the same cycle together.
34	
35	**Alternatives considered.** (A) Multi-run schema in one file: `{cycle_id, runs: [...]}`. Faithful, but every reader has to learn the new shape and the on-disk backup file needs migrating. (B) Open-and-extend: load the existing file and append to a single flat `entries` list. Loses run boundaries, and `cycle_duration_seconds` (computed as `max(finished_at) - min(started_at)`) spans the inter-run gap and becomes misleading. (D) Refuse to clobber: fail-fast if the file exists. Breaks the unattended re-run flow the tool exists for (fix X, re-run, drain the rest) until a resume mode is built.
36	
37	**Why per-run files.** The bug being fixed (ABA-230) is that a second invocation against the same cycle silently overwrites the first run's data. Per-run files make the write path single-writer-write-once — no read-then-write race, no schema diff, no migration. US-D (ABA-197), which already plans to glob `runs/*.json` and merge across cycles, gets a one-line addition (group by `cycle_id`) instead of a new shape to read. Each file's `cycle_duration_seconds` represents one invocation's hands-off time; US-D sums them when reporting cycle-level KR2.
38	
39	**Cost.** A re-run cycle accumulates one file per invocation. At cycle scale (≤ 15 issues, rarely more than 2–3 invocations to drain) this is negligible. No retention policy is shipped; trim by hand if it ever matters.
40	
41	## 5. Each issue declares its target repo via a `repo:<name>` Linear label
42	
43	The orchestrator used to be single-repo by construction: `repo = Path.cwd()`, every worktree under `<cwd>/.worktrees/`. Cycles in this workspace span multiple repos by design — `linear-workflow.md` makes "Affected repos" part of the six initiative-readiness fields, and the Ops slot deliberately holds cross-repo maintenance issues. Each issue now carries a `repo:<name>` Linear label; `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml` maps the name to an absolute path; the operator runs `drain-cycle` from anywhere.
44	
45	**Alternatives considered.**
46	
47	- *Description-body encoding* (e.g. a `Repo: drain-cycle` line in the markdown). The description is the most-edited surface, agents rewrite it routinely, and there's no schema enforcement. A label is one structured field with one value — Linear validates it, and a label rename triggers a clear "this label doesn't exist" error rather than silently drifting to a wrong repo.
48	- *Title prefix* (e.g. `[drain-cycle] Fix the foo`). Cleaner machine parse than the body, but every issue's title gains visual clutter for a parser-only concern. Labels don't pay that cost.
49	- *Inherit from the Linear project's "affected repos"*. Ambiguous for projects that legitimately touch multiple repos, and there is no obvious answer for the Ops container project, which is multi-repo by definition.
50	
51	**Missing-repo halt behaviour.** Same machinery as every other pre-spawn halt: write a run-log entry, print the `Halt:` line to stderr, exit 1, leave subsequent issues untouched. The new wrinkle is that resolution failures happen before any Linear state is moved, so they skip the post-spawn revert path (`_revert_to_pre_halt_state`). This is the difference from the existing setup-failure halt: that one happens after `worktree.add` or the initial `linear.set_state` attempt; the resolution halt happens before either. Either way, no revert is needed because no state was moved.
52	
53	**`repos.yml` config errors are even earlier.** A missing or malformed `repos.yml` halts the CLI at startup, before any Linear traffic and before the run-log file is created. There is no cycle yet to log against, so the failure surfaces only on stderr. This is enforced eagerly in `cli.main` so the orchestrator never sees a broken config.
54	
55	**Why labels not file convention** (e.g. requiring the operator to put the issue identifier in a branch comment, or use a remote-name convention). Labels are the only signal that's enforceable in Linear's UI: cycle planners can see at a glance which repo an issue targets, and the multi-repo distinction is visible at the right surface (Linear) rather than buried in a config file.
56	
57	**Out-of-v1 deliberately.** No env-var expansion inside `repos.yml`; no auto-clone if the path is missing; no parallelism across repos; no retroactive labelling of pre-cycle issues. All are operator-time concerns rather than tool-time concerns. Multi-team Linear support stays out of scope too — the tool is still hardcoded to the `Personal` team.
58	
59	## 6. Installed as a `uv tool`, with the secret read from `~/.drain-cycle/.env`
60	
61	`drain-cycle` is installed via `uv tool install`, which puts the executable on `$PATH` in an isolated environment. The Linear API key is read from `~/.drain-cycle/.env` (shell-exported vars still win), beside the `repos.yml` config and the `runs/` logs the tool already kept there.
62	
63	**Alternatives considered.**
64	
65	- *`pipx`*. Functionally equivalent for installing a Python CLI in isolation. Rejected because `uv` already anchors this repo's stack (`uv.lock`, the `mise.toml` Python pin) — adding `pipx` spends an innovation token on a second tool that does the same job.
66	- *Publish to PyPI*. Lets anyone `uv tool install drain-cycle` by name, but buys a release-and-versioning burden — tagging, changelogs, a name on the index — that a single-operator tool doesn't earn. `uv tool install git+https://…` already covers install-from-anywhere with no release step.
67	
68	**The secret-loading change this forced.** The CLI used to load `.env` only from the repo root (`Path(__file__).parent.parent`). Once installed, the package lives in the isolated tool env, where that path has no `.env` — so the key has to live somewhere stable. The load order is now shell env → `~/.drain-cycle/.env` → repo-root `.env`, first hit wins (`load_dotenv` defaults to `override=False`). The repo-root entry survives only as a dev-checkout fallback (it works under `--editable`, where `__file__` still points into the checkout); the installed tool reads the key from `~/.drain-cycle/.env`.
69	
70	**Out-of-v1 deliberately.** No PyPI release. No `drain-cycle init` command to scaffold `~/.drain-cycle/` — a missing `repos.yml` already halts with an actionable message that prints the expected shape, and `docs/repos.example.yml` is a copyable template, which is enough for a single operator.
71	
72	## Out of scope (deliberate)
73	
74	- **Parallelism.** Issues run one at a time. The Linear cycle is the unit; intra-cycle parallelism adds resource contention and serialises poorly with the agent-self-update pattern (two agents racing to mark different issues Done is fine, but two agents racing on overlapping files is not).
75	- **Retry.** A halted issue is not retried automatically. The operator inspects the worktree and decides — fix, redo manually, or descope.
76	- **Cross-cycle scheduling.** One cycle per invocation. Chaining cycles is an operator concern.
77	
```

id: toolu_0188x2diPQtRmfw2TVb5RFbm
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/design-decisions.md",
  "old_string": "## Out of scope (deliberate)",
  "new_string": "## 7. Workers default to Sonnet; a `model:` label overrides per issue\n\nA spawned `claude -p` worker inherits whatever model the operator has globally pinned. In the diagnosed quota-burn run all five workers ran on `claude-opus-4-7` (the operator's global pin), and Opus was the single largest cost multiplier of the ~108M-token spend. Workers now default to `claude-sonnet-4-6`, passed explicitly via `--model`; an individual issue opts up (or down) with a `model:<alias>` Linear label, mirroring the `repo:<name>` mechanism. Known aliases (`sonnet`/`opus`/`haiku`) map to full ids; an unrecognised value is passed to `claude --model` verbatim.\n\n**Why lenient, not strict.** Unlike `repo:` resolution — where a missing label is a hard halt because there is no safe default target — model resolution always has a safe fallback. So it never raises: no label, an unknown alias, or conflicting `model:` labels all fall back to the default rather than halting an unattended cycle over a label typo. The model actually used is recorded in the run log, so a mis-labelled issue surfaces after the fact instead of stalling the run.\n\n**Alternatives considered.** (A) Keep inheriting the global pin — rejected, it is exactly what caused the burn and gives the operator no per-issue control. (B) A single global `--model` flag with no per-issue override — simpler, but a cycle legitimately mixes cheap mechanical issues with a few that warrant Opus; per-issue is the right grain. (C) Raise on ambiguous labels like `repo:` does — rejected, halting a whole unattended cycle over a duplicate label is worse than silently taking the cheap, safe default.\n\n## Out of scope (deliberate)"
}
```

```
1	"""Drain-the-cycle iteration test for the orchestrator.
2	
3	The walking skeleton covered one issue end-to-end. This test pins the
4	behaviour that *every* sorted Todo/Backlog issue is processed in order, each
5	in its own worktree, and that every worktree is removed when the spawned
6	session signals completion.
7	
8	What we substitute and why:
9	
10	* Linear: stubbed in-process. The orchestrator imports the linear module by
11	  attribute, so monkey-patching ``cycle_id`` / ``pending_issues`` / ``get_issue``
12	  on it is sufficient. We do **not** stub the GraphQL transport — the layer
13	  under test here is the orchestrator loop, not the wire format (the wire
14	  format is exercised separately).
15	* Spawned ``claude -p``: replaced with a real shell script via
16	  ``_CLAUDE_CMD``. The script writes the basename of its cwd (the issue
17	  identifier — that's how the orchestrator names worktrees) into a shared
18	  marker file. The stubbed ``get_issue`` reads that file to decide which
19	  issues are Done. This satisfies the spec's "no-op script that calls back
20	  into the stubbed Linear" — the callback path is the marker file rather
21	  than an in-process call, because the script runs in a separate process.
22	* Git: a real ``git init`` repo with one commit on ``main``. ``git worktree``
23	  is exercised for real; this is the cheapest way to be sure the orchestrator
24	  doesn't paper over a worktree problem.
25	"""
26	from __future__ import annotations
27	
28	import subprocess
29	from pathlib import Path
30	
31	import pytest
32	
33	from drain_cycle import linear, orchestrator, repos
34	
35	
36	def _issue(
37	    identifier: str,
38	    priority: int,
39	    sort_order: float,
40	    *,
41	    repo_name: str = "test-repo",
42	) -> dict:
43	    return {
44	        "id": f"id-{identifier}",
45	        "identifier": identifier,
46	        "title": f"Title for {identifier}",
47	        "description": f"Body for {identifier}",
48	        "priority": priority,
49	        "sortOrder": sort_order,
50	        "state": {"type": "unstarted", "name": "Todo"},
51	        "labels": [f"repo:{repo_name}"],
52	    }
53	
54	
55	def _init_repo(repo: Path) -> None:
56	    """Create a real git repo with one commit on ``main`` for worktree tests."""
57	    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-b", "main"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
58	    subprocess.run(
59	        ["git", "config", "user.email", "test@example.com"], cwd=repo, check=True
60	    )
61	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.name", "Test"], cwd=repo, check=True)
62	    (repo / "README.md").write_text("seed\n")
63	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", "."], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
64	    subprocess.run(
65	        ["git", "commit", "-m", "seed"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True
66	    )
67	
68	
69	def test_orchestrator_drains_every_issue_in_sorted_order(
70	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
71	) -> None:
72	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
73	    repo.mkdir()
74	    _init_repo(repo)
75	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
76	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
77	
78	    # Three issues — chosen so a "naïve" iteration order (input order) and
79	    # the priority sort disagree, proving the loop consumes the *sorted*
80	    # list rather than the unsorted one.
81	    raw_issues = [
82	        _issue("ABA-X", priority=3, sort_order=2.0),  # Medium
83	        _issue("ABA-Y", priority=1, sort_order=1.0),  # Urgent — runs first
84	        _issue("ABA-Z", priority=2, sort_order=3.0),  # High
85	    ]
86	    sorted_issues = linear._sort_pending_issues(raw_issues)
87	    expected_order = [i["identifier"] for i in sorted_issues]
88	    assert expected_order == ["ABA-Y", "ABA-Z", "ABA-X"]
89	
90	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
91	    done_marker = tmp_path / "done-identifiers.txt"
92	
93	    def fake_current_cycle_id() -> str:
94	        return "stub-cycle"
95	
96	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
97	        # Re-sort each call (mirrors the real client, which sorts the wire
98	        # response) and exclude anything the fake claude script has already
99	        # marked Done — so an accidental re-fetch can't re-feed the same
100	        # issue back into the loop.
101	        completed = _completed_identifiers(done_marker)
102	        return linear._sort_pending_issues(
103	            [i for i in raw_issues if i["identifier"] not in completed]
104	        )
105	
106	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
107	        issue = issues_by_id[issue_id]
108	        if issue["identifier"] in _completed_identifiers(done_marker):
109	            return {**issue, "state": {"type": "completed", "name": "Done"}}
110	        return issue
111	
112	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", fake_current_cycle_id)
113	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
114	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
115	    # set_state is exercised by tests/test_orchestrator_set_state.py; here it's
116	    # a no-op so this test stays focused on iteration order + worktree cleanup.
117	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
118	
119	    fake_claude = _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path, done_marker)
120	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
121	
122	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={"test-repo": repo}))
123	
124	    assert exit_code == 0
125	    # The script appends the worktree-basename (== issue identifier) on each
126	    # run, so the file contents are the exact processing order.
127	    assert done_marker.read_text().splitlines() == expected_order
128	    # Every worktree was removed on success.
129	    worktrees_dir = repo / ".worktrees"
130	    assert not worktrees_dir.exists() or not any(worktrees_dir.iterdir())
131	    # ``git worktree list`` should show only the main checkout.
132	    listed = subprocess.run(
133	        ["git", "worktree", "list", "--porcelain"],
134	        cwd=repo,
135	        check=True,
136	        capture_output=True,
137	        text=True,
138	    ).stdout
139	    assert listed.count("worktree ") == 1
140	
141	
142	def _completed_identifiers(marker: Path) -> set[str]:
143	    if not marker.exists():
144	        return set()
145	    return {line for line in marker.read_text().splitlines() if line}
146	
147	
148	def _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path: Path, done_marker: Path) -> Path:
149	    """A no-op stand-in for ``claude -p``.
150	
151	    Records the basename of its cwd (the issue identifier — orchestrator
152	    names worktrees ``.worktrees/<identifier>/``) into ``done_marker``.
153	    The stubbed ``get_issue`` reads this file to learn which issues the
154	    'agent' has completed.
155	    """
156	    script = tmp_path / "fake-claude.sh"
157	    script.write_text(
158	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
159	        f'printf "%s\\n" "$(basename "$PWD")" >> "{done_marker}"\n'
160	    )
161	    script.chmod(0o755)
162	    return script
163	
```

> AGENT

The fake-claude script ignores argv entirely, so the acceptance criterion *"a `model:opus` issue runs the Opus alias"* — the orchestrator integration — is **not** covered by any test (only the resolver unit is). That's a Required gap per the review. Let me add an integration test that captures the spawned command's argv.

orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={\"test-repo\": repo}))\n    assert exit_code == 0\n\n    assert _model_arg(argv_dir / \"ABA-DEF.txt\") == \"claude-sonnet-4-6\"\n    assert _model_arg(argv_dir / \"ABA-OPUS.txt\") == \"claude-opus-4-7\"\n\n\ndef _model_arg(argv_file: Path) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the token following ``--model`` in a captured argv file.\"\"\"\n    argv = argv_file.read_text().splitlines()\n    return argv[argv.index(\"--model\") + 1]\n\n\ndef _write_argv_capturing_claude_script(\n    tmp_path: Path, done_marker: Path, argv_dir: Path\n) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"A ``claude -p`` stand-in that records its argv (one token per line)\n    to ``argv_dir/<identifier>.txt`` and marks the issue Done.\"\"\"\n    script = tmp_path / \"fake-claude-argv.sh\"\n    script.write_text(\n        \"#!/bin/sh\\n\"\n        'id=\"$(basename \"$PWD\")\"\\n'\n        f': > \"{argv_dir}/$id.txt\"\\n'\n        f'for a in \"$@\"; do printf \"%s\\\\n\" \"$a\" >> \"{argv_dir}/$id.txt\"; done\\n'\n        f'printf \"%s\\\\n\" \"$id\" >> \"{done_marker}\"\\n'\n    )\n    script.chmod(0o755)\n    return script\n\n\ndef _completed_identifiers(marker: Path) -> set[str]:"
